## 29. Subject Intelligence Pipeline


> **NEW IN v3.0** — This entire section documents the multi-subject AI intelligence pipeline that was built during Sprint 3. It is the cognitive backbone of AimPeak.


### 29.1 Architecture Overview


The intelligence pipeline generates production-grade seed data for the knowledge graph, micro-concepts, edges, and problem types. It runs as a **Python offline pipeline** that outputs JSON seed files consumed by the TypeScript seeder.


```
syllabus_intelligence.py  →  parse_syllabus.py  →  syllabus_physics_detailed.json (2.4 MB)
syllabus_intelligence_math.py  →  parse_syllabus_math.py  →  syllabus_math_detailed.json (960 KB)
                                                              ↓
                                                   seed/syllabus.ts → PostgreSQL
```


### 29.2 Physics Pipeline (V1)


| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Chapters | 22 |
| Topics | 368 |
| Micro-Concepts | 1,095 |
| Problem Types | Yes |
| Seed file | `src/lib/db/seed_data/syllabus_physics_detailed.json` (2.4 MB) |
| Engine | `syllabus_intelligence.py` (70 KB) |
| Parser | `parse_syllabus.py` (50 KB) |


### 29.3 Mathematics Pipeline (V2 — Two-Tier MC Architecture)


| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Chapters | 22 |
| Topics | 178 |
| Core Micro-Concepts | 523 (drive graph, BFS, FSRS) |
| Supplementary Micro-Concepts | 111 (diagnosis + personalization only) |
| Total Micro-Concepts | 634 |
| Edges | 523 (501 intra-chapter + 22 cross-chapter) |
| Core Orphans | 0 |
| Semantic Clusters | 9 |
| Transformation Tags | 5 |
| Seed file | `src/lib/db/seed_data/syllabus_math_detailed.json` (960 KB) |
| Engine | `syllabus_intelligence_math.py` (105 KB) |
| Parser | `parse_syllabus_math.py` (31 KB) |


#### Two-Tier MC Design


```
CORE MCs (523)          → Knowledge graph nodes, BFS traversal, FSRS scheduling
                         → Only core MCs create edges (graph stays clean)
                         → Examples: "definition_of_sets", "binomial_theorem_general_term"


SUPPLEMENTARY MCs (111) → AI diagnosis, granular weakness targeting
                         → Never pollute the knowledge graph
                         → Examples: "sign_handling_in_integration", "modulus_edge_cases"
```


#### Edge Generation (3-Strategy Algorithm)


1. **Sequential within-chapter**: Topic N → Topic N+1 (learning order)
2. **Intra-topic MC chains**: Core MCs within same topic linked sequentially
3. **Cross-chapter dependencies**: Via `ALLOWED_CROSS_DOMAIN_MATH` mapping (e.g., Trigonometry → Calculus)


#### 5 Transformation Tags


| Tag | Example |
|---|---|
| `algebraic_manipulation` | Factoring, expansion, simplification |
| `substitution_pattern` | Variable substitution in integrals |
| `identity_transformation` | Trig identities, algebraic identities |
| `reduction_strategy` | Reducing complex to simpler form |
| `problem_decomposition` | Breaking multi-step into sub-problems |


#### 9 Semantic Clusters


`foundational_algebra` · `number_theory_extensions` · `combinatorial_reasoning` · `sequence_pattern_analysis` · `matrix_linear_algebra` · `trigonometric_analysis` · `coordinate_geometry` · `calculus_core` · `probability_statistics`


#### Edge Intent Classification


| Intent | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `prerequisite` | Must learn A before B |
| `enhances` | A makes B easier but not required |
| `shortcut` | A provides alternative approach to B |


### 29.4 Chemistry Pipeline (V3 — 5-Layer Intelligence Architecture)


> **IMPLEMENTED: April 16–30, 2026** — Full 3-division chemistry pipeline with V3 graph intelligence upgrade.


| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Divisions | 3 (Physical / Organic / Inorganic) |
| Chapters | 32 |
| Topics | 286 |
| Micro-Concepts | 883 |
| KG Edges | 657 (8 semantic edge types) |
| Named Reactions | 36 |
| Mechanism Chains | 8 |
| Chemistry Exceptions | 18 |
| Memory Clusters | 6 |
| Conversion Pathways | 7 |
| Seed file | `src/lib/db/seed_data/syllabus_chemistry_detailed.json` |
| Engine | `syllabus_intelligence_chemistry.py` (90 KB) |
| Parser | `parse_syllabus_chemistry.py` (55 KB) |
| Seed script | `src/lib/db/seed/chemistry_v2.ts` (12 KB) |
| CLI runner | `src/lib/db/seed/run_chemistry_seed.ts` |


#### Architecture (5 Layers)


```
Layer 1: syllabus_intelligence_chemistry.py    → EXACT_MAPPINGS (800+ MCs), difficulty, tier, clusters
Layer 2: parse_syllabus_chemistry.py           → Parses raw text + generates 9-pass edge pipeline
Layer 3: syllabus_chemistry_detailed.json      → Full seed JSON (chapters, topics, MCs, edges, reactions, etc.)
Layer 4: chemistry_v2.ts                       → Idempotent TypeScript seeder → PostgreSQL (Neon)
Layer 5: v3_backfill.ts                        → Tier backfill for ALL subjects (system-wide property)
```


#### 3-Division Model


| Division | Chapters | Topics | Difficulty Drivers |
|---|---|---|---|
| Physical Chemistry | 12 | 112 | Equation depth, derivation complexity, graph interpretation |
| Organic Chemistry | 12 | 107 | Mechanism depth, stereo complexity, named reaction density |
| Inorganic Chemistry | 8 | 67 | Exception density, memory load, trick frequency |


#### V3 Graph Intelligence — 8 Edge Types


| Edge Type | Count | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `builds_on` | 441 | Intra-topic MC chains |
| `numerical_dependency` | 102 | Formula-to-formula MC links |
| `reaction_dependency` | 53 | Multi-MC reaction → concept links |
| `requires` | 30 | Cross-topic semantic MC matching |
| `exception_override` | 14 | Exception → trick/exception MC |
| `mechanism_step` | 8 | Mechanism step → governing concept MC |
| `conversion_path` | 6 | Conversion pathway step → concept MC |
| `memory_association` | 3 | Memory cluster → linked MC |


#### V3 Semantic MC Matching


Cross-topic edges use weighted scoring with hard cap (score < 3 → fallback):


| Priority | Weight | Signal |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | +10 | Exact code overlap (e.g. nernst_equation ↔ nernst) |
| 2 | +5 | Same concept_cluster |
| 3 | +3/tag | Tag overlap (formula↔formula, mechanism↔mechanism) |
| 4 | +2 | Keyword semantic match (MC_SEMANTIC_KEYWORDS dictionary) |


Hard cap: score < 3 → use first MC fallback (prevent noisy edges)


#### Tier System (System-Wide Property)


All MCs across ALL subjects (Physics + Math + Chemistry) now have `tier`:
- **`core`** — Drives BFS graph, FSRS scheduling, test selection
- **`supplementary`** — AI diagnosis, weakness targeting only


Tier assignment rules: `importance_weight >= 0.7` OR tags include formula/derivation/mechanism/named_reaction/trick/exception/rule → core. Everything else → supplementary.


#### Chemistry-Specific Data Structures


**Reaction Nodes** (36 entries) — Each includes `depends_on_mcs` linking to 3–5 specific concept MCs.


**Mechanism Chains** (8 entries) — Each step includes `linked_mc` for graph integration.


**Memory Clusters** (6 entries) — Each includes `linked_mc_code` for FSRS tracking.


**Conversion Pathways** (7 entries) — Multi-step organic conversion chains with step-level MC linking.


**Chemistry Exceptions** (18 entries) — JEE trick questions linked to exception/trick MCs.

| `notification.service.ts` | 3KB | In-app notification CRUD |


### V2 AI Intelligence Upgrades


#### aiTutor.service.ts — 5-Type Failure Detection


```
[THEOREM GAP]          → concept accuracy < 40% on theorem MCs
[TRANSFORMATION GAP]   → substitution/identity/algebraic manipulation errors
[STRATEGY GAP]         → conceptualRate > 30% with ≥5 attempts
[EXECUTION GAP]        → careless errors > 1.5x conceptual errors
[CONCEPTUAL GAP]       → default fundamental understanding deficit
```


#### questionSelector.service.ts — Subject-Aware Scoring


- Auto-detects math vs physics via chapter → subject JOIN
- Hard/very_hard math questions get **1.4x Fisher Information boost**
- Low mastery topics get 1.5x FI boost; mastered topics get 0.7x penalty


#### knowledgeGraph.service.ts — V2 Learning Path Engine


- **FSRS-decay mastery**: `effective_mastery = 0.6 * raw + 0.4 * retrievability`
- **BFS learning path chains**: Full prerequisite chains (Vectors → Kinematics → Kinetic Energy), not greedy 1-step
- **Impact-scored sorting**: `(1 - mastery) * importance` prioritization
- **Confidence calculator**: 3-factor (data quality, signal clarity, prereq coverage)
- **Depth penalty**: Longer chains → lower confidence recommendations

Final Vision (Locked In)
One line vision:
“NexusCode is the unified cockpit for a student developer’s life – practice, contests, hackathons, profile, college tribe, and jobs in one place.”
Core differentiators (from your research):
1. Unified Developer Identity
   * Aggregates Codeforces/LeetCode/etc + hackathons + projects + college verification.
   * Shows verified skills, not just “I know Python” text.
2. College-Centric Tribes & Rivalry
   * College verification, college leaderboards, rivalry loops.
   * This is your growth engine.
3. Event & Opportunity Aggregator
   * One feed for all: hackathons, fellowships, contests, programs, internships.
   * No more 10 tabs: Devpost + Unstop + random Google forms etc.
4. Secure, Scalable Judge + Serious Gamification
   * Judge0 / NSJail style secure judge.
   * Streaks, badges, college ranks – but tied to real skill and career outcomes.
These 4 things are your north star. Any feature idea -> check if it supports one of them. If no → park it for later.
________________


2. MVP Scope (What v1 must have)
You cannot build whole NexusCode in v1. MVP should:
2.1 Users & Profiles
* Sign up with:
   * Email + password
   * Optional: Google / GitHub later
* College verification (basic version):
   * Phase 1: whitelist just your college domain (e.g. @iiitl.ac.in).
   * Tag every user with college_id.
* Profile fields:
   * Name, year, branch.
   * Links: CF, CC, LC, GitHub, LinkedIn.
   * Simple stats area (we can fill later from external fetchers).
2.2 Coding & DSA Core
* Problem list (start with 50–200 problems):
   * Stored in your DB.
   * Tags: topic + difficulty.
* Online Judge (Phase 1):
   * Use self-hosted Judge0.
   * Support C++ + Python initially.
* Features:
   * Submit code → get verdict (AC, WA, TLE, RE).
   * Show attempts history per user.
* No need for rating system in MVP, just:
   * Problems solved count.
   * Basic per-user stats.
2.3 Events & Hackathon Feed (Basic Aggregator)
* Simple Events page with:
   * Title, type (Hackathon / Contest / Program / Fellowship),
   * Mode (Online/Offline),
   * Start/End date,
   * Link to external site,
   * Tags (AI, Web, CP, Cloud, etc).
* In MVP:
   * Fill this manually or via 1–2 simple scrapers.
   * Aim for: global famous stuff (GSoC, SIH, Hacktoberfest, MLH events, big hackathons).
No need for complete automation day 1 – you just need a useful, curated feed.
2.4 College Leaderboard + Gamification Base
* Global leaderboard:
   * Sorted by: score = problems_solved + bonus_for_events.
* College leaderboard:
   * same score, filter by college.
* Badges (simple):
   * “Solved 10 problems”
   * “Solved 3 days in a row”
   * “Joined first event”
* Streak:
   * track “days user solved at least 1 problem”.
2.5 Social Lite (Super Simple)
For MVP you don’t need full LinkedIn.
* A simple Activity feed:
   * “X solved 5 problems this week”
   * “Y registered for Z hackathon”
   * “Z unlocked ‘Graph Basics’ badge”
* Likes only. No comments/chat yet.
________________


3. Non-Goals for MVP (Important so you don’t drown)
These are explicitly NOT in MVP:
* Full chat system (world chat, regional chat, DMs).
* Team finder with matching algorithm.
* Recruiter dashboard & job portal.
* Complex AI recommender.
* Full auto-scraping of all events globally.
* Complex rating system like Codeforces.
* Mobile app (Native). Just make Web very good + responsive.
We add these later in phases. MVP = One solid core loop:
Login → Solve problems → See leaderboard + badges → Check events → Repeat.
________________


4. Architecture You Should Actually Build (Practical)
You don’t need full crazy microservices from day 1. Based on your research, best path:
4.1 Phase 1 Architecture (0–6 months) – Modular Monolith + 2 Extra Services
* Backend (Monolith):
   * Example: Node.js (NestJS) or Django/FastAPI.
   * Modules inside:
      * Auth & Users
      * Profiles & Colleges
      * Problems & Submissions (DB-side, not judge execution)
      * Events
      * Leaderboards & Badges
      * Activity Feed
* Judge service:
   * Judge0 deployed separately.
   * Your backend calls Judge0 via REST.
* Event worker:
   * Small Python service/cron that:
      * Either scrapes 1–2 sites or imports from a Google Sheet.
      * Writes into events table.
Use PostgreSQL as primary DB. Use Redis just for:
* caching leaderboards,
* storing streak counters,
* rate limiting.
Later you can split into full microservices once you hit scale.
________________


5. Data Model (High-Level)
Just the main tables you need now:
* users
   * id, name, email, hashed_password, college_id, branch, year, created_at, role.
* colleges
   * id, name, domain.
* profiles
   * user_id, bio, links (github, cf, cc, lc, linkedin).
* problems
   * id, title, description, difficulty, tags (array), created_by.
* submissions
   * id, user_id, problem_id, language, code, verdict, runtime_ms, memory_kb, created_at.
* events
   * id, title, type, mode, location, start_date, end_date, link, tags, source.
* user_stats
   * user_id, problems_solved, current_streak, longest_streak, last_solve_date, total_submissions.
* badges
   * id, name, condition_type, condition_value.
* user_badges
   * id, user_id, badge_id, awarded_at.
* activities
   * id, user_id, type (SOLVE, BADGE, EVENT_JOIN), metadata(json), created_at.
Good enough to power MVP.
________________


6. Phased Roadmap (Refined from your research)
Phase 1 (0–3 months) – Foundation + Core Loop
Goal: One college, genuinely useful for daily coding & event discovery.
Build:
* Auth + college verification (single domain).
* Profiles + simple stats.
* Problem list + integration with Judge0.
* Submissions + verdicts.
* Leaderboard (global + college).
* Activity feed.
* Events page (manual/semimanual input).
When this works for your college → you already have something demo-able.
________________


Phase 2 (4–6 months) – Community & Light Intelligence
* Add:
   * Basic chat: per-college group chat only.
   * Team “space” on event detail page (people can click “Looking for team”).
   * Simple recommendation: “Popular problems this week”, “Upcoming events in next 7 days”.
   * Lightweight external stats: e.g., fetch Codeforces rating via API and show on profile.
Start onboarding 2–3 more colleges manually (friends, contacts).
________________


Phase 3 (7–12 months) – Multi-college, Recruiter-ready
* Full college onboarding flow (domains list, email verify).
* More event sources + scheduled scrapers.
* Better gamification (seasons, bigger college vs college wars).
* Recruiter side (beta):
   * Simple talent search (by problems solved, college, badges).
* First sponsored hackathon with a company / local startup.

   * Copy the MVP section above into a doc & mark any feature you absolutely want to add/remove.

   * Frontend: React / Next.js.
   * Backend: Pick one (NestJS or Django/FastAPI).

   * Bring 10–20 friends.
   * Run a small contest or “DSA week” using your own platform.
   * Observe what breaks / what they like.

# AETHER Backend


Production backend for AETHER Club - IIIT Lucknow.


## Tech Stack


- **Runtime**: Node.js + TypeScript
- **Framework**: Express.js
- **Database**: PostgreSQL (via Docker)
- **ORM**: Prisma
- **Authentication**: JWT
- **Payments**: Razorpay
- **Storage**: Cloudinary


## Quick Start


### 1. Prerequisites


- Node.js >= 18
- Docker & Docker Compose
- pnpm/npm/yarn


### 2. Setup


```bash
# Navigate to backend
cd backend


# Install dependencies
npm install


# Copy environment file
cp .env.example .env
# Edit .env with your actual values


# Start PostgreSQL (Docker)
docker-compose up -d


# Generate Prisma client
npm run db:generate


# Run migrations
npm run db:migrate


# Seed database (creates admin user)
npm run db:seed


# Start development server
npm run dev
```


### 3. Environment Variables


```env
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://aether:aether_secret@localhost:5432/aether_db
JWT_ACCESS_SECRET=your_secret_key_min_32_chars
JWT_REFRESH_SECRET=your_refresh_key_min_32_chars
RAZORPAY_KEY_ID=rzp_test_xxx
RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET=xxx
RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET=xxx
```


## API Routes


### Public
| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| GET | `/api/health` | Health check |
| GET | `/api/wings` | List all wings |
| GET | `/api/team` | Get club team |
| GET | `/api/symposium/active` | Get active symposium |
| GET | `/api/events/:symposiumId` | List events |


### Auth
| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| POST | `/api/auth/register` | Register user |
| POST | `/api/auth/login` | Login |
| POST | `/api/auth/refresh` | Refresh tokens |
| POST | `/api/auth/logout` | Logout |
| GET | `/api/auth/me` | Current user |


### Protected (Requires Auth)
| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| POST | `/api/registration/create` | Register for event |
| GET | `/api/registration/my` | My registrations |
| GET | `/api/registration/status/:id` | Check status |
| POST | `/api/payments/create-order` | Create Razorpay order |
| GET | `/api/payments/verify/:orderId` | Verify payment |


### Admin (Requires Admin Role)
| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|
| POST | `/api/admin/wing` | Create wing |
| POST | `/api/admin/team` | Add team member |
| POST | `/api/admin/symposium` | Create symposium |
| POST | `/api/admin/event` | Create event |
| GET | `/api/admin/registrations` | All registrations |


## Payment Flow


1. Frontend calls `POST /api/payments/create-order`
2. Backend creates Razorpay order, stores with PENDING status
3. User completes payment on Razorpay checkout
4. Razorpay sends webhook to `POST /api/payments/webhook`
5. Backend verifies signature, updates status to SUCCESS/FAILED
6. Frontend polls `GET /api/registration/status/:id`


> ⚠️ **CRITICAL**: Frontend NEVER marks payment as success. Only webhook updates DB.


## Scripts


```bash
npm run dev          # Development server
npm run build        # Build for production
npm run start        # Start production server
npm run db:generate  # Generate Prisma client
npm run db:migrate   # Run migrations
npm run db:seed      # Seed database
npm run db:studio    # Open Prisma Studio
```


## Project Structure


```
backend/
├── src/
│   ├── config/         # DB, env, Razorpay, Cloudinary
│   ├── middlewares/    # Auth, admin, error handling
│   ├── modules/        # Feature modules
│   │   ├── auth/
│   │   ├── users/
│   │   ├── wings/
│   │   ├── team/
│   │   ├── symposium/
│   │   ├── events/
│   │   ├── registration/
│   │   ├── payments/
│   │   └── admin/
│   ├── utils/          # JWT, logger, validators
│   ├── routes.ts       # Route aggregation
│   ├── app.ts          # Express app
│   └── server.ts       # Entry point
├── prisma/
│   ├── schema.prisma   # Database schema
│   └── seed.ts         # Admin seeding
└── docker-compose.yml  # PostgreSQL
```

## Pre-Deployment Checklist


Before deploying to production:


- [ ] Verify production `.env` file contains all required variables
- [ ] Set `NODE_ENV=production`
- [ ] Configure production database URL
- [ ] Set up Razorpay live keys (not test)
- [ ] Configure Cloudinary for production
- [ ] Set up email service (Resend)
- [ ] Configure CORS for production domain
- [ ] Set up SSL/HTTPS
- [ ] Run `npx prisma migrate deploy` on production DB

## Environment Variables Required


```bash
# Required for Production
NODE_ENV=production
DATABASE_URL=<production_postgres_url>
JWT_ACCESS_SECRET=<min_32_char_secret>
JWT_REFRESH_SECRET=<min_32_char_secret>
RAZORPAY_KEY_ID=<live_key_id>
RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET=<live_key_secret>
RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET=<webhook_secret>
CLOUDINARY_CLOUD_NAME=<cloud_name>
CLOUDINARY_API_KEY=<api_key>
```

# AETHER Payment & Email Audit Report


**Date:** 2026-01-17
**Status:** ✅ PASSED


## Payment Security


### Razorpay Integration
| Check | Status |
|-------|--------|
| KEY_SECRET server-side only | ✅ |
| WEBHOOK_SECRET server-side only | ✅ |
| Webhook signature verification | ✅ |
| Payment status from webhook only | ✅ |


### Payment Flow
1. Frontend creates order via `/api/payments/create-order`
2. Backend generates Razorpay order with KEY_ID + KEY_SECRET
3. Frontend receives order_id + KEY_ID only (no secret)
4. User completes payment in Razorpay modal
5. Razorpay sends webhook to `/api/payments/webhook`
6. Backend verifies webhook signature using WEBHOOK_SECRET
7. Only on valid signature: Update payment status


### Webhook Implementation
```typescript
// backend/src/modules/payments/payment.controller.ts
- Uses crypto.createHmac('sha256', webhookSecret)
- Verifies X-Razorpay-Signature header
- Rejects requests that don't match
```


## Email Security


### Email Configuration
| Check | Status |
|-------|--------|
| RESEND_API_KEY server-side only | ✅ |
| No email credentials in frontend | ✅ |


### Email Trigger Points
| Trigger | When |
|---------|------|
| Payment Success | After webhook confirms payment |
| Registration Confirmed | After payment success only |
| Pending Reminder | Cron job (not spam - checks status) |


### Reminder Job Safety
```typescript
// backend/src/jobs/pendingReminder.job.ts
- Queries only PENDING registrations
- Checks last reminder time
- Won't resend within 24 hours
- Logs all sent reminders
```


### Email Templates
- React Email (type-safe)
- No sensitive data in templates
- Dynamic data sanitized


## Conclusion
**Payment and email security properly implemented. No sensitive data exposed to frontend.**

## 📖 Overview


AETHER is a full-stack web platform for managing technical symposiums, club activities, and event registrations for the Data Science & AI/ML Club at IIIT Lucknow.


### ✨ Key Features


- **🎪 Symposium 2026** - Event showcase, registration, and ticketing
- **💳 Payment Integration** - Razorpay-powered payment processing
- **📧 Email Notifications** - Automated confirmation and reminder emails
- **🖼️ Asset Management** - Cloudinary-based image uploads with cropping
- **👥 Team Showcase** - Club coordinators, faculty, and symposium team
- **🦋 Wings Management** - WnC (Web & Coding) and Climate Tech wings
- **🔐 Admin Dashboard** - Complete content management system


---

### Frontend
| Technology | Purpose |
|------------|---------|
| React 18.3 | UI Framework |
| Vite 6.0 | Build Tool |
| React Router 7 | Routing |
| Framer Motion | Animations |
| Lucide Icons | Icon Library |
| CSS (Vanilla) | Styling |


### Backend
| Technology | Purpose |
|------------|---------|
| Node.js | Runtime |
| Express.js | HTTP Server |
| Prisma 6.0 | ORM |
| PostgreSQL | Database |
| JWT | Authentication |
| Razorpay | Payments |
| Cloudinary | Image Storage |
| React Email + Resend | Transactional Emails |

## 🚀 Quick Start


### Prerequisites
- Node.js 18+
- PostgreSQL 16+ (or Docker)
- npm or yarn


### 1. Clone & Install


```bash
# Clone repository
git clone https://github.com/your-org/aether.git
cd aether


# Install frontend dependencies
npm install


# Install backend dependencies
cd backend
npm install
```


### 2. Environment Setup


```bash
# Copy environment template
cp backend/.env.example backend/.env


# Edit with your values
# See .env.example for all required variables
```


### 3. Database Setup


```bash
cd backend


# Run Prisma migrations
npx prisma migrate dev


# Seed initial data
npx prisma db seed
```


### 4. Start Development


```bash
# Terminal 1 - Backend
cd backend
npm run dev


# Terminal 2 - Frontend
cd ..
npm run dev
```


- Frontend: http://localhost:5173
- Backend API: http://localhost:5000/api
- Admin: http://localhost:5173/admin

src/feature_geneartor.py
import random


def generate_feature_vector():
    # Simulate 78 features between 0.5 to 1.0 (like confidence scores or normalized values)
    return [round(random.uniform(0.5, 1.0), 2) for _ in range(78)]

src/infer.py
import requests
import time
import numpy as np


def generate_random_features():
    return np.random.rand(78).tolist()


def send_to_api(features):
    try:
        response = requests.post("http://127.0.0.1:5001/predict", json={"features": features})
        if response.status_code == 200:
            result = response.json()
            print(f"Detected Class: {result['predicted_class']} | Confidence: {result['confidence']}")
        else:
            print("Prediction error:", response.text)
    except Exception as e:
        print("API call failed:", e)


def run_simulation(interval=5):
    while True:
        features = generate_random_features()
        send_to_api(features)
        time.sleep(interval)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_simulation()


src/preprocess.py
import os
import pandas as pd
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler
import mlflow
import numpy as np


DATA_DIR = "data/raw"
OUTPUT_DIR = "data/processed"
TARGET_CLASSES = ["BENIGN", "DoS Hulk", "DDoS", "PortScan"]


def load_dataset():
    all_files = [f for f in os.listdir(DATA_DIR) if f.endswith(".csv")]
    df_list = []


    for file in all_files:
        print(f"Reading {file}")
        df = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(DATA_DIR, file), low_memory=False)
        df_list.append(df)


    combined_df = pd.concat(df_list, ignore_index=True)
    return combined_df


def main():
    mlflow.set_experiment("CyberThreatPreprocessing")
    with mlflow.start_run():
        mlflow.autolog()


        print("Loading data...")
        df = load_dataset()
        print("Initial shape:", df.shape)


        df.replace([np.inf, -np.inf], np.nan, inplace=True)
        df.dropna(inplace=True)
        print("After removing NaNs/Infs:", df.shape)


        if " Label" in df.columns:
            df.rename(columns={" Label": "Label"}, inplace=True)
        elif "Label" not in df.columns:
            raise ValueError("'Label' column not found in data!")


        df = df[df["Label"].isin(TARGET_CLASSES)]
        label_map = {"BENIGN": 0, "DoS Hulk": 1, "DDoS": 2, "PortScan": 3}
        df["Label"] = df["Label"].map(label_map)


        # Drop non-numeric columns just in case
        df = df.select_dtypes(include=["number"]).copy()


        X = df.drop("Label", axis=1)
        y = df["Label"]


        scaler = StandardScaler()
        X_scaled = scaler.fit_transform(X)


        df_scaled = pd.DataFrame(X_scaled, columns=X.columns)
        df_scaled["Label"] = y.values


        os.makedirs(OUTPUT_DIR, exist_ok=True)
        output_path = os.path.join(OUTPUT_DIR, "cleaned_data.csv")
        df_scaled.to_csv(output_path, index=False)
        print(f"Saved cleaned & normalized data to {output_path}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

src/train.py
import os
import pandas as pd
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset, random_split
import mlflow
import mlflow.pytorch
import numpy as np


mlflow.set_experiment("CyberThreatModelTraining")


# Model definition
class SimpleNN(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, input_dim):
        super(SimpleNN, self).__init__()
        self.net = nn.Sequential(
            nn.Linear(input_dim, 128),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Dropout(0.3),
            nn.Linear(128, 64),
            nn.ReLU(),
            nn.Linear(64, 4)  # 4 classes
        )


    def forward(self, x):
        return self.net(x)


def load_data(path):
    df = pd.read_csv(path)
    X = df.drop(columns=["Label"]).values
    y = df["Label"].values


    X_tensor = torch.tensor(X, dtype=torch.float32)
    y_tensor = torch.tensor(y, dtype=torch.long)


    dataset = TensorDataset(X_tensor, y_tensor)
    train_size = int(0.8 * len(dataset))
    val_size = len(dataset) - train_size


    return random_split(dataset, [train_size, val_size])


def train_model(train_loader, val_loader, input_dim):
    model = SimpleNN(input_dim)
    criterion = nn.CrossEntropyLoss()
    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=0.001)


    for epoch in range(10):
        model.train()
        total_loss = 0
        for X_batch, y_batch in train_loader:
            optimizer.zero_grad()
            outputs = model(X_batch)
            loss = criterion(outputs, y_batch)
            loss.backward()
            optimizer.step()
            total_loss += loss.item()


        print(f"Epoch [{epoch+1}/10], Loss: {total_loss:.4f}")


    # Evaluation
    model.eval()
    correct = 0
    total = 0
    with torch.no_grad():
        for X_batch, y_batch in val_loader:
            outputs = model(X_batch)
            _, predicted = torch.max(outputs.data, 1)
            total += y_batch.size(0)
            correct += (predicted == y_batch).sum().item()


    accuracy = 100 * correct / total
    print(f"Validation Accuracy: {accuracy:.2f}%")
    return model, accuracy, train_loader


def main():
    with mlflow.start_run():
        mlflow.autolog()


        train_dataset, val_dataset = load_data("data/processed/cleaned_data.csv")
        train_loader = DataLoader(train_dataset, batch_size=1024, shuffle=True)
        val_loader = DataLoader(val_dataset, batch_size=1024)


        input_dim = train_dataset[0][0].shape[0]


        model, accuracy, train_loader = train_model(train_loader, val_loader, input_dim)


        # Prepare input_example (convert torch.Tensor to numpy array)
        input_example = train_dataset[0][0].unsqueeze(0).numpy()


        # Log validation accuracy and the trained model to MLflow
        mlflow.log_metric("val_accuracy", accuracy)
        mlflow.pytorch.log_model(model, "cyber_model", input_example=input_example)


        print(f"Model saved to MLflow with accuracy {accuracy:.2f}%")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

  <script>
    async function predictThreat() {
      const input = document.getElementById("features").value;
      const resultDiv = document.getElementById("result");
      const loader = document.getElementById("loader");
      resultDiv.innerHTML = "";


      let features = input.split(",").map(Number);
      if (features.length !== 78 || features.some(isNaN)) {
        resultDiv.innerHTML = "⚠️ Please enter exactly 78 valid numeric features.";
        return;
      }


      loader.style.display = "block";


      try {
        const res = await fetch("http://127.0.0.1:5001/predict", {
          method: "POST",
          headers: {
            "Content-Type": "application/json",
          },
          body: JSON.stringify({ features }),
        });


        const data = await res.json();
        loader.style.display = "none";


        if (data.error) {
          resultDiv.innerHTML = `❌ Error: ${data.error}`;
        } else {
          resultDiv.innerHTML = 
`🛡️ Predicted Class: ${data.predicted_class}
📈 Confidence: ${(data.confidence * 100).toFixed(2)}%


📋 Threat Log:
- Source: Internal system scan
- Time: ${new Date().toLocaleTimeString()}
- Analysis: ${data.predicted_class === "Attack" ? "⚠️ Potential cyber threat detected." : "✅ No threat detected."}`;
        }
      } catch (err) {
        loader.style.display = "none";
        resultDiv.innerHTML = `❌ Request failed. Check if your server is running.`;
      }
    }


    function simulateInput() {
      const arr = Array.from({ length: 78 }, () => (Math.random() * 100).toFixed(2));
      document.getElementById("features").value = arr.join(",");
    }
  </script>
</body>
</html>


app.py
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
import mlflow.pytorch
from flask import Flask, request, jsonify, render_template
import os
import requests  # Add this import


# Initialize Flask app
app = Flask(__name__)


# Ensure templates folder exists for HTML
app.template_folder = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "templates")


# Path to your MLflow model
model_uri = "mlruns/571955796731090884/cdd0faded6bc474db131e8950c8e1a5d/artifacts/cyber_model"


# Load the model
model = mlflow.pytorch.load_model(model_uri)
model.eval()  # Set model to evaluation mode


# Global variable to store latest result
latest_result = {"predicted_class": "-", "confidence": "-"}


@app.route("/")
def home():
    return render_template("index.html")  # Loads the front-end page


@app.route("/predict", methods=["POST"])
def predict():
    data = request.get_json()


    if not data or "features" not in data:
        return jsonify({"error": "Missing 'features' key in JSON input"}), 400


    features = data["features"]


    if len(features) != 78:
        return jsonify({"error": f"Expected 78 features, got {len(features)}"}), 400


    try:
        input_tensor = torch.tensor(features, dtype=torch.float32).unsqueeze(0)


        with torch.no_grad():
            outputs = model(input_tensor)
            probabilities = F.softmax(outputs, dim=1)
            predicted_class = torch.argmax(probabilities, dim=1).item()
            confidence = torch.max(probabilities).item()


        # Send the latest result to the update_result endpoint
        requests.post("http://127.0.0.1:5001/update_result", json={
            "predicted_class": int(predicted_class),
            "confidence": round(confidence, 4)
        })


        return jsonify({
            "predicted_class": int(predicted_class),
            "confidence": round(confidence, 4)
        })


    except Exception as e:
        return jsonify({"error": str(e)}), 500


@app.route("/update_result", methods=["POST"])
def update_result():
    global latest_result
    data = request.get_json()
    latest_result = data
    return jsonify({"status": "updated"}), 200


@app.route("/latest", methods=["GET"])
def get_latest_result():
    return jsonify(latest_result)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    app.run(debug=True, port=5001)

IoT Machine Learning/AI Robotic Process Automation
gpt-oss-120b and gpt-oss-20b are here. Developed with feedback from the open-source community, these models push the frontier of open models at their size. 
Hugging Face, NVIDIA, Ollama, vLLM, LM Studio and OpenAI have come together to host this six week hackathon. Your challenge is to apply these models in creative, unexpected ways: build a robot with reasoning, craft a local agent that thrives offline, fine-tune a model into something indispensable, or push the edge with the weirdest hardware you can imagine. Maybe your project will benefit humanity, or maybe it’ll be so unexpected it surprises everyone in the room. However you build, this is your moment to redefine what open models can do.
 
Get Started
   * Both gpt-oss models are free to download on Hugging Face.
   * Learn more about the models: openai.com/open-models
   * Github: github.com/openai/gpt-oss 
   * Check out this microsite of gpt-oss in action: gpt-oss.com
Requirements
What to Build
Apply the gpt-oss models in the most interesting way. Surprise us with something we haven’t seen before! Orient your application into the following categories—we’ll pick one winner for each:
   * Best Overall — The absolutely most interesting application of gpt-oss.
   * Best In Robotics — The most effective use of gpt-oss models in an IRL robot. (Record a video of it in action!)
   * Weirdest Hardware — The most creative and interesting hardware product that uses gpt-oss models.
   * Best Local Agent — The most useful agentic application of gpt-oss with no internet access.
   * Most Useful Fine-Tune — The most effective use of gpt-oss for specialized domains, through fine-tuning.
   * Wildcard — The most unexpected use of gpt-oss that OpenAI didn’t think of.
   * For Humanity — The best application of gpt-oss that benefits all of humanity.
 
What to Submit
   1. Choose the category or categories that best aligns with your project and provide a brief explanation for your choice.
   2. Text description that explains the features and functionality of your Project.
   3. <3-minute Demonstration video of your Project
   4. Provide a URL to your public code repository for judging and testing, with clear indication of gpt-oss model use (e.g., README with testing instructions, sample data, or a link to model weights / training data if you fine-tuned). An OSS/Apache License is encouraged, but not required.
Demotivator AI
Demotivator AI : ruthless, sarcastic, and painfully honest. We strip your ego, trash your tactics, and deliver the one thing people avoid : brutal clarity


Inspiration
Most hackathon projects aim to motivate, inspire, or “change the world.” We decided to do the opposite. We were inspired by: Toxic startup culture - where every half-baked project is hyped as “world-changing.” Motivational fluff -99% of AI “life coaches” are just ChatGPT with pom-poms. Our own frustration - sometimes the most useful feedback is just: 𝑌𝑜𝑢𝑟 𝐼𝑑𝑒𝑎 = Garbage That painful hit of honesty is what Demotivator AI delivers.
What It Does
Demotivator AI roasts you, crushes your ego, and exposes every flaw in your ideas.No pep talks.No sugarcoating.No fake positivity.If your startup is a donkey dressed as a unicorn, we’ll call it a donkey. Brutally. Honestly. Sarcastically.
How We Built It
Model Backbone: gpt-oss-20b, one of the largest open models. Quantization: Tried mxfp4, AWQ, even considered GPTQ to squeeze the beast into VRAM. Serving: Used vLLM for OpenAI-compatible APIs. Backend: FastAPI + Uvicorn to handle requests. Frontend: Minimalistic terminal/optional UI — because the roast, not the interface, is what hurts. Code snippet of roast delivery:
@app.post("/roast") async def roast_user(input_text: str): response = openai.ChatCompletion.create( model="gpt-oss-20b", messages=[ {"role": "system", "content": "You are a brutal demotivator AI."}, {"role": "user", "content": input_text} ] ) return {"roast": response["choices"][0]["message"]["content"]}
Challenges We Ran Into
CUDA Out of Memory
   torch.OutOfMemoryError: Tried to allocate 1.08 GiB


Translation: buy a bigger GPU.
Disk Storage Hell Downloaded the 20B model ~7 times. Nearly nuked our C: drive. Quantization Myths Papers said “20B fits on 8GB VRAM.” Reality said “LOL, no.” Tone Balance Making it funny brutal, not depressing brutal.
Accomplishments That We’re Proud Of
Actually got gpt-oss-20b running on our setup (after multiple GPU meltdowns). Built a fast roast pipeline with vLLM + FastAPI.Managed to turn sarcasm into a product.Accepted that sometimes deleting 50GB of failed checkpoints is progress.
What We Learned
Big models are cruel — in VRAM, disk, and words. WSL eats space like a black hole — learned to nuke cache directories properly. Hackathon coding = therapy — half the time debugging CUDA errors, the other half laughing at roasts. Sarcasm is addictive — once you build a roast bot, you never want to build a cheerleader again.
What’s Next for Demotivator AI
Fine-tuning on startup pitch datasets — so it can roast like a seasoned VC. Add voice mode — because being roasted out loud hurts more. Roast-as-a-service (RaaS) — integrate into pitch practice, career coaching, even dating apps. Demotivation Leaderboard — rank the most destroyed egos at the hackathon.
Closing Thought
Most AI projects pretend to “change the world.” Ours doesn’t. Demotivator AI exists for one purpose:
To remind you that your idea probably sucks. But hey — at least now you know why.

import torch
from diffusers import StableDiffusionControlNetPipeline, ControlNetModel
from diffusers.utils import load_image
import gradio as gr
import time




try:
    print("⚙️ Loading optimized models...")
    start_time = time.time()




    controlnet = ControlNetModel.from_pretrained(
        "lllyasviel/control_v11p_sd15_scribble",
        torch_dtype=torch.float16
    )




    pipe = StableDiffusionControlNetPipeline.from_pretrained(
        "runwayml/stable-diffusion-v1-5",
        controlnet=controlnet,
        torch_dtype=torch.float16,
        safety_checker=None
    ).to("cuda")




    pipe.enable_attention_slicing()


    load_time = time.time() - start_time
    print(f"✅ Models loaded in {load_time:.1f}s - Ready to generate!")


except Exception as e:
    print(f"❌ Critical error: {str(e)}")
    print("🔧 Try: Runtime → Factory reset runtime → Reinstall packages")
    raise




def generate_image(doodle, prompt):
    start_time = time.time()
    try:


        result = pipe(
            prompt=prompt + ", high quality",  # Improves results
            image=doodle,
            num_inference_steps=20,
            guidance_scale=7.0,
            height=512,
            width=512
        )
        gen_time = time.time() - start_time
        print(f"Generated in {gen_time:.1f}s")
        return result.images[0]
    except Exception as e:
        return f"🚨 Error: {str(e)}"




with gr.Blocks() as app:
    gr.Markdown("# 🎨 Doodle-to-Image Converter")


    with gr.Row():
        with gr.Column():
            sketch = gr.Image(label="1. Upload your doodle", type="pil")
            prompt = gr.Textbox(label="2. Describe what you want",
                              value="a realistic version of this doodle")
            btn = gr.Button("3. Generate (20-40s)", variant="primary")
        output = gr.Image(label="Generated Image")


    btn.click(
        fn=generate_image,
        inputs=[sketch, prompt],
        outputs=output
    )




app.launch(share=True, server_port=7860, server_name="0.0.0.0")

🌍 UN Sustainable Development Goal Alignment
A.P.E.X directly addresses UN SDG 9: Industry, Innovation, and Infrastructure:
SDG 9 Target
	How A.P.E.X Contributes
	9.1 — Develop sustainable, resilient infrastructure
	Transforms India's existing toll infrastructure into an intelligent monitoring network; builds freight resilience through predictive AI routing
	9.4 — Upgrade infrastructure for sustainability
	Reduces fuel waste from unnecessary delays; cuts CO₂ emissions by preventing cascade congestion events
	9.c — Universal access to ICT
	Leverages existing FASTag (RFID) infrastructure — no new hardware required, works with 5.9 crore active tags
	A.P.E.X also supports SDG 11 (Sustainable Cities & Communities) through reduced urban freight congestion and SDG 13 (Climate Action) through optimized routing that minimizes fuel consumption.

🎯 Problem Statement
India's logistics costs consume 7.97% of GDP (₹22.6 lakh crore annually) — significantly higher than the global average of 6.7% — according to the DPIIT-NCAER Assessment of Logistics Cost in India, 2023-24. For smaller firms (turnover ≤₹5 crore), this burden reaches 16.9% of output.
Despite India's improvement to 38th on the World Bank Logistics Performance Index (2023), highway freight — which carries 65% of India's total freight — still operates reactively:
The Problem
	Scale
	Daily FASTag transactions
	10.5 million (105 lakh/day, FY2025-26)
	Active FASTags nationwide
	5.9 crore (59 million)
	Annual toll collection
	₹61,408 crore (FY 2024-25)
	Avg toll-plaza crossing time
	40 seconds (ETC) vs 12 min (manual)
	Data being used for prediction
	0% — all data is for billing only
	The core insight: India already has 10.5 million GPS-like data points per day from FASTag transactions. This data stream is being used only for billing. A.P.E.X transforms it into a real-time predictive intelligence network.
________________

│        │          Event Log Ring Buffer (deque)            │             │
│        │    Real-time state sync + cache invalidation      │             │
│        └───────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘             │
│                                │                                         │
│                   ┌────────────┴────────────┐                            │
│                   │    Firebase RTDB        │                            │

│                   └─────────────────────────┘                            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


Data Flow — Disruption → AI Response
1. User injects disruption (or auto-detected)
         │
2. XGBoost classifies → CRITICAL (96% confidence)
         │
3. graph_nodes updated in-memory → cache invalidated
         │
4. A* computes optimal reroute (15 nodes, 21 edges)
         │
5. Event logged to ring buffer → SSE broadcast
         │
6. Gemini 2.5 Flash generates structured analysis
         │
7. Frontend reflects disruption in < 100ms

	Auto-scaling, sub-3s cold start, serverless

	Sub-100ms reads, live sync across clients

	Firebase Hosting
	Frontend SPA deployment with global CDN
	SSL, CDN, custom domain support
	6

	Base map layer with Deck.GL overlay

	Secure, key-less authentication in production

🤖 Gemini 2.5 Flash — Deep Integration
A.P.E.X uses Gemini 2.5 Flash via Vertex AI (google.genai SDK) for three tightly integrated AI capabilities:
#
	Use Case
	Endpoint
	Description
	1
	Disruption Analysis
	POST /analyze-disruption
	Root cause identification, cascade risk assessment, recommended actions — structured JSON
	2
	Natural Language Query
	POST /gemini-query
	"What's the riskiest corridor?" → Parses intent, queries live graph state, returns risk level + relevant nodes
	3
	Predictive Insights
	GET /gemini-insights
	Continuous network monitoring with warnings, predictions, and optimization suggestions
	Real-Time Context Intelligence
Gemini responses are always aware of the current network state through:
      1. In-memory state sync — graph_nodes dictionary updated on every disruption injection
      2. Event log ring buffer — deque(maxlen=50) tracks recent disruptions, fed into every prompt
      3. Cache invalidation — Gemini response cache cleared instantly on state changes
      4. Enriched prompts — Every query includes current node statuses + last 10 events
Why Gemini 2.5 Flash over Pro? See ADR-002 — Flash provides structured JSON output with ~4s latency vs Pro's ~12s, critical for real-time agent loops.
________________


🧠 ML Inference Pipeline
Models
Model
	Task
	Latency
	Details
	XGBoost
	Disruption classification (CRITICAL/MODERATE/LOW)
	<15ms
	Binary classifier, 96% confidence on test data
	Random Forest
	Risk scoring (0.0–1.0)
	<10ms
	Ensemble of 100 trees, multi-feature input
	A Router*
	Optimal reroute computation
	<50ms
	NetworkX graph, 15 nodes, 21 edges, multi-objective
	Gemini 2.5 Flash
	NL analysis + predictions
	~4–8s
	Structured JSON via Vertex AI
	Cascade Engine
The cascade propagation engine uses a Motter-Lai inspired model:
Disruption at Node A
       ↓
Impact propagated to neighbors (decay = 0.95)
       ↓
Utilization redistributed across graph
       ↓
XGBoost re-classifies all affected nodes
       ↓
A* finds optimal bypass route
       ↓
Gemini generates human-readable analysis


________________


🧮 Mathematical Foundations
Model
	Formula
	Application
	Queueing Theory
	M/M/1: ρ = λ/μ, bottleneck at ρ ≥ 0.85
	Node congestion detection
	BPR Delay
	t = t₀[1 + α(V/C)^β], α=0.88, β=9.8
	Indian-calibrated traffic delay
	Motter-Lai Cascade
	C_j = (1+α)·L_j(0)
	Cascade failure propagation
	Resilience Metric
	SSW = max(0, TTS − TTR)
	Node survival safety window
	Privacy
	SHA-256(vehicle_id)
	DPDPA 2023 compliance
	________________

🚀 Quick Start
Prerequisites
      * Node.js 18+
      * Python 3.11+
      * Google Cloud SDK (for deployment)
      * A Google Cloud Project with Vertex AI enabled
1. Clone
git clone https://github.com/AAYUSH-SPIDEY-SHARMA/A.P.E.X.git
cd A.P.E.X
2. Backend Setup
cd backend/processor


# Install dependencies
pip install -r requirements.txt


# Configure environment
cp .env.example .env
# Edit .env with your credentials:
#   GEMINI_API_KEY=your-key
#   FIREBASE_DATABASE_URL=your-firebase-url
#   DEMO_MODE=true


# Set Python path
export PYTHONPATH=$(pwd)/../..   # Linux/Mac
$env:PYTHONPATH="$(pwd)\..\.."   # Windows PowerShell


# Run
uvicorn main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8080
3. Frontend Setup
cd frontend


# Install dependencies
npm install


# Configure environment
cp .env.example .env
# Edit .env with your Firebase + Google Maps credentials


# Run
npm run dev
Open http://localhost:5174 to see the dashboard.
4. Cloud Deployment
# Backend → Cloud Run
gcloud run deploy apex-ml-agent \
    --source . \
    --port 8080 \
    --region asia-south1 \
    --memory 2Gi --cpu 2 \
    --min-instances 1 --cpu-boost \
    --timeout 3600 --concurrency 80 \
    --allow-unauthenticated \
    --set-env-vars "GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=your-project,GCP_LOCATION=asia-south1,DEMO_MODE=true"


# Frontend → Firebase Hosting
cd frontend && npm run build
firebase deploy --only hosting

🏆 Key Differentiators
      1. Zero New Hardware — Uses India's existing FASTag infrastructure (5.9 Cr active tags)
      2. Autonomous AI Response — XGBoost + A* + Gemini pipeline runs without human intervention
      3. Real-Time AI Context — Gemini responses reflect live network state via event log buffer + cache invalidation
      4. 60fps Digital Twin — Production-grade WebGL visualization with Google Maps + deck.gl
      5. Indian-Calibrated Mathematics — BPR α=0.88, β=9.8 from NHAI toll plaza data (not generic Western values)
      6. DPDPA 2023 Compliant — SHA-256 pseudonymization of vehicle IDs
      7. 7 Google Technologies — Gemini, Vertex AI, Cloud Run, Firebase (RTDB + Hosting), Google Maps, Cloud IAM
      8. Production-Grade Security — Vertex AI service account auth, zero API keys in deployed code
      9. Validated Against Real Data — Calibrated against NHAI FY2024-25 toll transaction volumes

## ⚡ ARCHITECTURE SUMMARY — WHAT WAS ACTUALLY BUILT


> This blueprint has been updated to reflect the **production system as built**. All code references point to actual files in the repository.


**Live Production Pipeline:**


```text
FASTag Simulator (Python) → Cloud Pub/Sub → Cloud Run (FastAPI + XGBoost + A* Router)
                                                    ↓                    ↓
                                              Firebase RTDB         SSE Stream
                                                    ↓                    ↓
                                           React + deck.gl v9 Dashboard
                                           (60fps rAF + Google Routes API HD Polylines)
```


**4 core services. Zero unnecessary complexity.**


**ML Models Deployed**: XGBoost (delay classification) + Random Forest (risk scoring) — loaded in-process inside Cloud Run FastAPI.


**Routing Engine**: Custom multi-objective A* with Haversine heuristic, M/M/1 queueing penalties, and ASI-weighted edge costs.


**Frontend**: React 19 + deck.gl v9 (WebGL) + Google Maps JS API + Zustand state management + Web Workers for polyline simplification.

# 1. PROJECT OVERVIEW & VISION


## 1.1 What Is A.P.E.X?


A.P.E.X is an **autonomous, self-healing supply chain digital twin** purpose-built for India's highway freight network. It continuously ingests real-time telemetry from India's Digital Public Infrastructure (FASTag toll plazas, eWay Bill transactions, ULIP APIs), predicts cascading congestion using XGBoost ML models with graph-based feature engineering, and autonomously reroutes freight using a custom multi-objective A* routing engine — all without human intervention, in under 5 seconds.


**A.P.E.X is NOT a fleet tracker.** It is an autonomous macro-economic nervous system that:
- **Predicts** disruptions 4–48 hours before they physically manifest using XGBoost + Random Forest ensemble
- **Simulates** cascading impact across the entire highway network via a real-time Digital Twin with Motter-Lai cascade modeling
- **Heals** the supply chain by autonomously executing optimized rerouting through an integrated AI agent — zero human intervention


## 1.2 Why India? Why Roadways?


India's supply chain presents a **uniquely challenging** computational problem:


| Factor | India Reality | Global Benchmark |
|--------|-------------|-----------------|
| Logistics cost as % of GDP | **13–14%** | 8% (developed nations) |
| Road freight dominance | **64–74%** of all freight | Japan: 10%, China: 30%, USA: 37% |
| Trucking fragmentation | **3.5 million operators**, avg 5–6 trucks each | Consolidated fleets in Western markets |
| GPS telematics adoption | **Near zero** in unorganized sector | Standard in organized logistics |
| Empty truck miles | **40%** of all truck miles are empty | ~15% globally |
| Road cost multiplier | **4–5x** more expensive than rail per tonne-km | — |


**The core insight**: India's unorganized trucking sector (75% of all operators own <5 trucks) has **no GPS tracking**. But India has FASTag — a **97% penetration** RFID toll collection system that generates timestamped, geolocated pings at every toll plaza. This is our primary sensor network.


## 1.3 The Paradigm Shift


| Aspect | Legacy Systems ("System of Record") | A.P.E.X ("System of Response") |
|--------|-------------------------------------|----------------------------------|
| **Detection** | After delay happens | 4–48 hours before manifestation |
| **Response** | Human-driven, manual | Fully autonomous AI agent |
| **Data** | Siloed (TMS, WMS, ERP) | Unified via ULIP + FASTag + eWay Bill |
| **Routing** | Static, daily batch | Dynamic, real-time A* optimization |
| **Scope** | Single enterprise | Cross-enterprise network intelligence |
| **India-specific** | Generic global solutions | DPI-native (FASTag, ULIP, Gati Shakti) |


## 1.4 Problem Statement (Official)


> **#3 Smart Supply Chains — Resilient Logistics and Dynamic Supply Chain Optimization**
>
> *"Design a scalable system capable of continuously analyzing multifaceted transit data to preemptively detect and flag potential supply chain disruptions. Formulate dynamic mechanisms that instantly execute or recommend highly optimized route adjustments before localized bottlenecks cascade into broader delays."*


**Our implementation**: An AI-powered digital twin of India's highway freight network using government DPI (FASTag, ULIP, eWay Bill) as its sensor layer, predicting cascading congestion via XGBoost + graph-based feature engineering, and autonomously healing the supply chain through a unified AI agent running on Cloud Run.


---


# 2. PROBLEM DOMAIN — INDIA'S HIGHWAY FREIGHT CRISIS


*(Sections 2.1 through 2.5 remain unchanged — these are domain facts, not implementation details)*


## 2.1 The Indian Freight Lifecycle


A typical domestic freight shipment in India follows this path:


```
Manufacturing Plant → Factory Loading → Regional Highway Transit →
Toll Plazas (FASTag) → State Border Crossings (RTO Checks) →
ICD/MMLP Transshipment → Secondary Distribution → Last-Mile Delivery
```


**Critical friction points unique to India**:
- **RTO checkposts**: Regional Transport Office inspections cause 2–9 hour delays at state borders
- **Toll plaza congestion**: Despite 97% FASTag penetration, peak-hour queues cause massive delays
- **ICD dwell times**: ICD Tughlakabad averages 116 hours export dwell time
- **Monsoon disruptions**: Western Ghats, Konkan Railway, and NH-48 are severely weather-vulnerable


## 2.2 Modal Imbalance — Why Road Dominates


| Transport Mode | Modal Share | Cost per Tonne-Km | Status |
|---------------|-------------|-------------------|--------|
| **Road** | 60–64% | ₹3.78 (4–5x baseline) | Highly congested trunk routes |
| **Rail** | 27–31% | ₹1.96 (baseline) | Capacity >100% on heavy routes |
| **Coastal/Waterways** | 5–9% | ₹2.30 | Underutilized; Sagarmala |
| **Air** | <1% | ₹72.00 (36x baseline) | Urgent, high-value only |


## 2.3 The Fragmentation Crisis


| Metric | Value |
|--------|-------|
| Total commercial trucks | ~12.5 million |
| Total truck operators | ~3.5 million |
| Average fleet size | 5–6 vehicles per operator |
| Firms with <5 trucks | 75% of all operators |
| GPS/telematics adoption | Near zero in unorganized sector |
| Empty running rate | 40% of all truck miles |
| Market value | ~$170 billion (FY2024) |


**Implication for our system**: We **cannot** rely on continuous GPS streams. We use **FASTag RFID toll plaza pings** as discrete edge-node events, then mathematically interpolate location, velocity, and ETA using the Haversine formula.


## 2.4 Why This Problem Is Computationally Hard


| Factor | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| **Non-linear delays** | A 2-hour RTO delay can cascade into multi-day disruptions |
| **Astronomical scale** | 12.5M trucks × thousands of toll plazas = billions of routing permutations |
| **Extreme uncertainty** | Monsoons, strikes, RTO inspections, fuel price volatility |
| **Real-time constraints** | Rerouting must execute in <5 seconds end-to-end |
| **Data sparsity** | Discrete FASTag pings, not continuous GPS |
| **Regulatory friction** | eWay Bill expiration deadlines, overloading penalties |


## 2.5 The Ripple Effect — Cascading Delay Propagation


In scale-free networks (power-law degree distribution), highly connected hubs have **exponentially amplified probability** of experiencing AND propagating delays. India's highway network exhibits exactly this topology — small-world properties with clustering coefficient ~0.78.


---


# 3. REAL-WORLD FAILURE SCENARIOS — INDIA-SPECIFIC


## 3.1 Scenario Matrix


*(Unchanged — these are domain scenarios)*


| Scenario | Current Reality | Systemic Failure | Decision Window |
|----------|----------------|------------------|-----------------|
| **Western Ghats Monsoon** | Heavy rainfall triggers landslides on NH-48 | Delhi-Mumbai corridor severed | 48–72 hours pre-event |
| **RTO Checkpoint Gridlock** | State border inspections cause 2–9 hour delays | No prediction capability | 15–30 minutes post-arrival |
| **ICD Tughlakabad Congestion** | Export dwell averages 116 hours | No upstream visibility | 24–48 hours pre-arrival |
| **ICEGATE System Failure** | EDI failures prevent customs payments | Manual workaround takes days | Immediate on failure |


## 3.2 The Killer Demo Scenario — Dual-Shock Disruption (AS IMPLEMENTED)


This is the **live demo scenario** implemented in the system:


### Shock 1: Western Ghats Monsoon (Meteorological)
- Extreme monsoon rainfall triggers severe landslides
- NH-48 physically severed, Western DFC impacted
- **Implemented**: `handleDualShock()` in `App.jsx` calls `injectAnomaly({ type: 'MONSOON', severity: 0.95, lat: 17.5, lng: 73.8 })`


### Shock 2: ICEGATE Glitch (Administrative)
- Customs portal EDI failures
- ICD Tughlakabad dwell time spikes to 300+ hours
- **Implemented**: Second injection with `{ type: 'ICEGATE_FAILURE', severity: 1.0, lat: 28.509, lng: 77.275 }`


### How A.P.E.X Resolves This (As Built)


1. **Detection**: ML Agent (Cloud Run) receives anomaly → XGBoost computes disruption probability → SSE pushes update to frontend
2. **Prediction**: Forward Risk propagation calculates cascade impact across all connected nodes
3. **Routing**: A* router generates optimal alternate paths avoiding blocked corridors, weighted by time + toll + ASI risk
4. **Visualization**: deck.gl arcs animate from blocked path (red) to rerouted path (green) in real-time at 60fps
5. **Impact Modal**: CascadeComparison component shows before/after with measured response time
6. **Resolution**: Supply chain healed autonomously — zero human intervention


**Actual measured response time**: `performance.now()` tracked from dual-shock trigger to reroute completion.

## 6.3 Our Innovation Stack (As Built)


1. **FASTag as Sensor Network**: 97% penetration RFID toll system as decentralized IoT — interpolating velocity from discrete toll pings using Haversine formula
2. **XGBoost + Random Forest Ensemble**: Dual-model prediction with graph-based feature engineering from NetworkX highway topology — fast, interpretable, production-grade
3. **Digital Twin with TTR/TTS/SSW**: Resilience engineering metrics quantifying exactly when and where the supply chain will break
4. **Autonomous Self-Healing Agent**: Single Cloud Run FastAPI service that independently detects, predicts, routes, and publishes — zero human intervention
5. **60fps GPU-Accelerated Visualization**: deck.gl v9 WebGL rendering with Google Routes API HD polylines, Douglas-Peucker simplification via Web Workers, and Zustand dual-cadence state management
6. **India DPI Integration**: First system to unify FASTag + ULIP + eWay Bill + PM Gati Shakti into a single predictive intelligence layer

# 8. SOLUTION ARCHITECTURE — GCP STACK (AS BUILT)


## 8.1 System Overview — Actual Production Architecture


```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           TIER 1: DATA INGESTION                        │
│  FASTag Simulator (Python async) → Cloud Pub/Sub        │
│  Mock DPI APIs (ULIP, Vahan, eWay Bill)                 │
│                    ↓                                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│           TIER 2: ML AGENT (Cloud Run)                  │
│  FastAPI + XGBoost + Random Forest (in-process)         │
│  A* Routing Engine (NetworkX graph)                     │
│  SSE Streaming + Firebase RTDB Writer                   │
│  Weather Service Integration                            │
│              ↓                    ↓                     │
├──────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Firebase RTDB   │  SSE Event Stream                    │
│  (Persistent     │  (Real-time ML                       │
│   State Sync)    │   Updates)                           │
├──────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┤
│           TIER 3: FRONTEND                              │
│  React 19 + Vite 8 + deck.gl v9 (WebGL)                │
│  Google Maps JS API (Dark Mode)                         │
│  Google Routes API (HD Polylines)                       │
│  Zustand State + Web Workers                            │
│  60fps rAF Animation Engine                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```


## 8.2 Google Cloud Platform Services (Actually Used)


| Layer | GCP Service | Purpose | Status |
|-------|-------------|---------|--------|
| **Ingestion** | Cloud Pub/Sub | Async message bus for FASTag telemetry | ✅ **BUILT** |
| **Compute** | Cloud Run | Single FastAPI container with ML models + A* router | ✅ **BUILT** |
| **Real-time DB** | Firebase RTDB | WebSocket-based real-time state sync | ✅ **BUILT** |
| **Maps** | Google Maps JS API | Base map with dark mode + deck.gl overlay | ✅ **BUILT** |
| **Routes** | Google Routes API | HD polyline fetching for realistic truck path rendering | ✅ **BUILT** |
| **Auth** | Firebase Auth | Service account authentication | ✅ **BUILT** |


**Services NOT deployed** (documented for production roadmap):
- Cloud Dataflow (Apache Beam) — stream processing at scale
- Spanner Graph — global-scale graph database
- BigQuery — historical analytics warehouse
- Vertex AI — managed ML model serving
- GKE — Kubernetes orchestration
- Memorystore Redis — sub-ms caching
- Route Optimization API — `batchOptimizeTours`


## 8.3 End-to-End Data Flow (As Built)


```
FASTag Simulator (backend/simulator/)
    ↓ (Cloud Pub/Sub "fastag-telemetry-stream")
Cloud Run ML Agent (backend/processor/main.py)
    ├── Pub/Sub subscriber → velocity calculation (Haversine)
    ├── XGBoost delay prediction (in-process, joblib.load)
    ├── Random Forest risk scoring (in-process)
    ├── A* routing engine (ml/routing/astar_router.py)
    ├── Motter-Lai cascade propagation (in-process)
    ├── Firebase RTDB writer (supply_chain/*)
    └── SSE stream (/stream endpoint)
         ↓                              ↓
    Firebase RTDB               SSE EventSource
         ↓                              ↓
    React Dashboard (frontend/src/App.jsx)
    ├── useFirebase.js (dual-source: Firebase + SSE)
    ├── useAnimatedFleet.js (60fps rAF animation)
    ├── useRoutePolylines.js (Routes API + IndexedDB cache)
    ├── fleetStore.js (Zustand dual-cadence state)
    └── deck.gl v9 layers (MapView.jsx)
         ├── ScatterplotLayer (network nodes)
         ├── PathLayer (corridor polylines)
         ├── IconLayer (animated truck icons)
         ├── HeatmapLayer (congestion heat)
         └── RadarSweepLayer (rerouted truck rings)
```


## 8.4 Latency Budget (Measured)


| Stage | Target | Actual | Service |
|-------|--------|--------|---------|
| Anomaly injection → ML Agent | <200ms | ~100ms | Cloud Run FastAPI |
| XGBoost inference | <100ms | ~15ms | In-process joblib |
| A* route calculation | <500ms | ~50-200ms | NetworkX + custom heuristic |
| Firebase RTDB write | <200ms | ~150ms | Firebase Admin SDK |
| SSE → React state update | <100ms | ~50ms | EventSource API |
| deck.gl layer rebuild | <100ms | ~30ms | WebGL GPU rendering |
| **Total end-to-end** | **<5 seconds** | **~2-4 seconds** | — |


## 8.5 Communication Protocols (As Built)


| Service Pair | Protocol | Implementation |
|---------|----------|-----|
| Frontend ↔ ML Agent | REST (HTTPS) + SSE | `fetch()` for commands, `EventSource` for streaming |
| Frontend ↔ Firebase | WebSocket | Firebase SDK `onValue` real-time listeners |
| Frontend ↔ Google Routes API | REST (HTTPS) | `routeService.js` with IndexedDB cache |
| ML Agent ↔ Firebase | REST (Admin SDK) | `firebase-admin` Python SDK |
| ML Agent ↔ Pub/Sub | gRPC (SDK) | `google-cloud-pubsub` Python SDK |

# 10. DATA MODELS & SCHEMAS (AS BUILT)


## 10.1 Highway Graph (As Built)


> The highway graph is represented as a **NetworkX DiGraph** loaded from `backend/graph/indian_highway_graph.json`, with nodes and edges loaded via `ml/routing/graph_loader.py`.


**Graph topology**:
- **Nodes**: Toll plazas, warehouses, ICDs, RTO checkpoints across Golden Quadrilateral
- **Edges**: Highway segments (NH-48, NH-44, SH-17, DFC-WESTERN, COASTAL-SAGARMALA, etc.)
- **Edge weights**: Multi-objective — distance (km), live velocity (km/h), toll cost (INR), risk score (0-1), ASI
- **Loaded at Cloud Run startup** — no external graph database needed


## 10.2 FASTag Telemetry Payload Schema


*(Unchanged — see original Section 10.2)*


## 10.3 eWay Bill Schema


*(Unchanged — see original Section 10.3)*


## 10.4 Firebase RTDB Structure (As Built)


```json
{
  "supply_chain": {
    "active_routes": {
      "<route_id>": {
        "truckId": "TRK-001",
        "vehicleRegNo": "MH04AB1234",
        "originCoordinates": [77.1025, 28.7041],
        "destinationCoordinates": [72.8777, 19.0760],
        "currentPosition": [76.5, 25.3],
        "status": "NORMAL | DISRUPTED | REROUTED",
        "isRerouted": false,
        "cargoValueINR": 850000,
        "ewayBillNo": 3410987654,
        "ewayBillExpiry": "<dynamic: futureExpiry(3.2)>",
        "eta": "2026-04-07T14:00:00Z",
        "riskScore": 0.23,
        "corridor": "NH-48",
        "commodity": "Auto Parts",
        "velocityKmh": 65
      }
    },
    "nodes": {
      "<node_id>": {
        "type": "TOLL_PLAZA | WAREHOUSE | ICD | RTO_CHECKPOINT",
        "name": "Kherki Daula Toll Plaza",
        "lat": 28.4167,
        "lng": 77.0500,
        "status": "NORMAL | DELAYED | DISRUPTED",
        "utilization": 0.65,
        "queueLength": 42,
        "tts": 72,
        "ttr": 48,
        "isLive": false
      }
    },
    "anomalies": {
      "<anomaly_id>": {
        "type": "MONSOON | FLOOD | ACCIDENT | RTO_GRIDLOCK | ICEGATE_FAILURE | FUEL_SHORTAGE",
        "lat": 19.0760,
        "lng": 72.8777,
        "severity": 0.9,
        "affectedHighway": "NH-48",
        "timestamp": "2026-04-27T10:00:00Z"
      }
    },
    "alerts": {
      "<alert_id>": {
        "message": "CRITICAL: Kherki Daula TTS (36h) < TTR (96h). SSW = 60h. A* rerouted 12 trucks to SH-17.",
        "severity": "CRITICAL | WARNING | INFO",
        "costSavedINR": 420000,
        "timestamp": "2026-04-27T10:05:00Z"
      }
    }
  }
}
```


**Key changes from original blueprint**:
- `ewayBillExpiry` is now **dynamically generated** via `futureExpiry()` — always 0.8-7.5 hours in the future
- Added `corridor` and `commodity` fields per route
- Added `isLive` flag on nodes (indicates ML Agent is driving this node's state via SSE)
- Added `FUEL_SHORTAGE` as anomaly type
- `velocityKmh` added to route objects


## 10.5 Mock Data (As Built)


Located in `frontend/src/data/mockData.js`:


- **15 trucks** with realistic Indian vehicle registrations (MH, GJ, RJ, DL, TN, UP, KA, HR, AP, WB prefixes)
- **15 nodes** including Kherki Daula, Panipat, JNPT Gate 3, ICD Tughlakabad, Walayar Checkpoint, etc.
- **Corridors**: NH-48, NH-44, NH-44-EAST-ALT
- **eWay Bill expiry dates**: Dynamically generated relative to `Date.now()` using `futureExpiry(hours)` utility
- **Commodities**: Auto Parts, Electronics, Textiles, Pharma, Steel Coils, Chemicals, etc.


## 10.6 Route Corridor Data (As Built)


Located in `frontend/src/data/routeWaypoints.js`:


**8 highway corridors** with dual data source strategy:


| Corridor ID | Route | Source | Points |
|-------------|-------|--------|--------|
| `NH-48` | Delhi → Mumbai (via Jaipur, Udaipur, Ahmedabad) | Google Routes API (HD) | ~40,000 |
| `SH-17-ALT` | Alternate coastal route | Google Routes API (HD) | ~35,000 |
| `NH-44` | Delhi → Chennai (via Agra, Hyderabad) | Google Routes API (HD) | ~45,000 |
| `NH-44-EAST-ALT` | Eastern alternate via Kolkata | Google Routes API (HD) | ~38,000 |
| `NH-48-SOUTH` | Mumbai → Bangalore | Google Routes API (HD) | ~30,000 |
| `NH-44-SOUTH` | Hyderabad → Chennai | Google Routes API (HD) | ~20,000 |
| `DFC-WESTERN` | Dadri → JNPT (Dedicated Freight Corridor) | Google Routes API (HD) | ~25,000 |
| `COASTAL-SAGARMALA` | Mumbai → Mangalore (Sagarmala coastal) | Google Routes API (HD) | ~22,000 |


**Architecture**:
- **Primary**: Google Routes API fetches HD polylines with `HIGH_QUALITY` encoding
- **Cache**: IndexedDB with 24-hour TTL — prevents repeat API calls
- **Fallback**: Dense static waypoints (80-200 points per corridor) embedded in `routeWaypoints.js`
- **Simplification**: Douglas-Peucker algorithm (ε=0.0005°) via Web Worker — reduces 40K→3K points for PathLayer rendering while keeping full resolution for truck animation


---


# 11. MACHINE LEARNING PIPELINE (AS BUILT)


## 11.1 Model Architecture Overview


| Model | Architecture | Primary Function | Status |
|-------|-------------|-----------------|--------|
| **Delay Classification** | XGBoost (scikit-learn) | Binary: will this node experience >60 min delay? | ✅ **DEPLOYED** — in-process on Cloud Run |
| **Risk Scoring** | Random Forest (scikit-learn) | Regression: predict risk score (0-1) per highway segment | ✅ **DEPLOYED** — in-process on Cloud Run |
| **Cascade Propagation** | Motter-Lai analytical model | Calculate cascading failure probability across network | ✅ **BUILT** — in `main.py` |
| **Anomaly Detection** | Rule-based + XGBoost | Detect bottlenecks: ρ > 0.85 threshold + XGBoost confirmation | ✅ **BUILT** |


**Models NOT deployed** (documented for production roadmap):
- ST-GNN (Spatio-Temporal Graph Neural Network) — requires Vertex AI
- BiLSTM (Bidirectional LSTM) — corridor congestion prediction
- GAT (Graph Attention Network) — ETA prediction
- Autoencoder + Isolation Forest — black swan detection


## 11.2 XGBoost Delay Prediction (As Built)


**Features used** (tabular, extracted from highway graph):
- `queue_length` — current trucks waiting at node
- `utilization` (ρ) — arrival rate / service rate
- `weather_severity` — normalized 0-1 from weather service
- `processing_rate` (μ) — vehicles processed per minute
- `node_degree` — NetworkX degree centrality
- `betweenness_centrality` — graph structural importance


**Training**: Offline using synthetic data generated from `ml/data/generate_synthetic.py`
**Serving**: `joblib.load('xgboost_model.pkl')` at Cloud Run startup — ~15ms inference latency
**Threshold**: Prediction probability > 0.7 triggers autonomous rerouting


## 11.3 Random Forest Risk Scoring (As Built)


**Same feature set as XGBoost** but regression output (continuous 0-1 risk score per edge).


Used by the A* router as `risk_score` edge weight — higher risk edges are penalized in path cost calculation.


## 11.4 Model Loading (As Built)


```python
# backend/processor/main.py — loaded at Cloud Run container startup
import joblib


xgb_model = joblib.load('ml/models/xgboost_delay_model.pkl')
rf_model  = joblib.load('ml/models/rf_risk_model.pkl')


# Inference: ~15ms per prediction
prediction = xgb_model.predict_proba(features)[0][1]  # P(delay > 60min)
risk_score = rf_model.predict(features)[0]              # Risk 0-1
```

# 12. CORE CODE IMPLEMENTATION (AS BUILT)


## 12.1 Backend — Cloud Run ML Agent


**File**: `backend/processor/main.py` (~1,700 lines)


**Architecture**: Single FastAPI application that serves as the unified ML Agent:


```python
# Key components:
class BoundedDict:
    """O(1) LRU cache using OrderedDict — prevents unbounded memory growth"""
    # Caps at MAX_SIZE entries, evicts oldest on overflow


app = FastAPI(title="A.P.E.X ML Agent")


# Endpoints:
@app.get("/health")          # System health check
@app.get("/ml-status")       # ML model status (XGBoost loaded, RF loaded, graph stats)
@app.post("/predict")        # XGBoost prediction for node features
@app.post("/inject-anomaly") # Disruption injection → cascade calculation → rerouting
@app.post("/demo/dual-shock")# Scripted dual-shock scenario for judges
@app.get("/stream")          # SSE endpoint for real-time ML updates to frontend
```


**Key capabilities**:
- **Dynamic O-D selection**: Automatically selects optimal origin-destination pairs for rerouting based on disrupted corridors
- **Dense utilization calculation**: Computes ρ for all affected nodes after disruption injection
- **SSE streaming**: Server-Sent Events push autonomous detection events (`AUTO_DETECTED`) to frontend
- **BoundedDict LRU**: Prevents memory leaks with capped dictionaries for prediction cache


## 12.2 FASTag Telemetry Simulator


**File**: `backend/simulator/fastag_simulator.py`


*(Implementation matches original blueprint Section 12.2 — async Python with Pub/Sub publisher)*


## 12.3 A* Routing Engine (As Built)


**File**: `ml/routing/astar_router.py` (~335 lines)


```python
class AStarRouter:
    """
    Multi-objective A* with India-specific cost function.
   
    f(n) = g_distance(n) + g_delay(n) + g_ASI(n) + h_haversine(n, goal)
   
    Edge cost = w1 * (distance / velocity)     # Travel time
              + w2 * toll_cost                  # Financial cost
              + w3 * risk_score                 # Safety (ASI-weighted)
              + w4 * M/M/1 queueing penalty    # Congestion at destination node
    """
   
    def find_route(self, origin, destination, blocked_edges=None):
        """Returns ordered waypoint list avoiding blocked corridors."""
       
    def find_alternative_routes(self, origin, destination, k=3):
        """K-shortest paths using Yen's algorithm."""
       
    @staticmethod
    def haversine(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2):
        """Admissible heuristic — straight-line distance (never overestimates)."""
```


**Graph loader**: `ml/routing/graph_loader.py` loads `backend/graph/indian_highway_graph.json` into NetworkX DiGraph.


## 12.4 Weather Service


**File**: `backend/processor/weather_service.py`


Integrates with weather APIs to provide real-time severity scores for route risk calculation.

# 13. OPTIMIZATION ALGORITHMS & ROUTING ENGINE (AS BUILT)


## 13.1 Algorithm Stack


| Algorithm | Status | Use Case | Complexity |
|-----------|--------|----------|-----------|
| **A* Search (custom)** | ✅ **DEPLOYED** | Multi-objective tactical routing | O(b^d) with Haversine heuristic |
| **Yen's K-Shortest Paths** | ✅ **DEPLOYED** | Generate k alternative routes | O(kn(m + n log n)) |
| **Haversine Distance** | ✅ **DEPLOYED** | Admissible heuristic + velocity interpolation | O(1) |
| **M/M/1 Queueing** | ✅ **DEPLOYED** | Bottleneck detection (ρ > 0.85) | O(1) per node |
| **Motter-Lai Cascade** | ✅ **DEPLOYED** | Cascading failure probability | O(V + E) per iteration |


**Not deployed** (production roadmap):
- MILP (OR-Tools/PuLP) — full constraint optimization
- ACO (Ant Colony) — multi-modal routing
- NSGA-III — multi-objective fleet optimization
- GraphHopper — self-hosted routing engine


## 13.2 Custom A* Heuristic (As Implemented)


```python
# ml/routing/astar_router.py
def edge_cost(self, u, v, data, blocked_edges):
    if (u, v) in blocked_edges:
        return float('inf')  # Blocked corridor — infinite cost
   
    distance = data.get('distance_km', 10)
    velocity = max(data.get('velocity_kmh', 40), 5)  # Velocity clip: min 5 km/h
    toll     = data.get('toll_cost_inr', 0)
    risk     = data.get('risk_score', 0)
    asi      = data.get('asi_score', 0)
   
    # M/M/1 queueing penalty at destination node
    rho = self.graph.nodes[v].get('utilization', 0.5)
    queue_penalty = rho / (1.0001 - rho) if rho < 1 else 100
   
    return (
        self.w_time * (distance / velocity) +      # Travel time (hours)
        self.w_cost * (toll / 10000) +              # Normalized toll cost
        self.w_risk * (risk + asi * 0.001) +         # Safety risk
        self.w_queue * queue_penalty * 0.1           # Congestion penalty
    )
```

# 14. DIGITAL TWIN ENGINE (AS BUILT)


## 14.1 What Is the Digital Twin?


The Digital Twin is the **live React + deck.gl frontend** backed by Firebase RTDB + SSE real-time state. It maintains a live mirror of every toll plaza, ICD, warehouse, and highway segment, displaying:
- Real-time truck positions animated at 60fps along HD polyline corridors
- Node status (NORMAL/DELAYED/DISRUPTED) with utilization gauges
- TTR/TTS/SSW resilience metrics per node (NodeInspector panel)
- Cascading failure visualization (nodes pulsing red as cascade propagates)
- Before/after impact comparison (CascadeComparison modal)


## 14.2 Disruption Injection Console (As Built)


**File**: `frontend/src/components/AnomalyConsole/AnomalyConsole.jsx`


Supports 4 injection modes:


| Mode | What It Does | Endpoint |
|------|-------------|----------|
| **Single Injection** | Judge selects anomaly type + severity → injects one disruption | `POST /inject-anomaly` |
| **Dual-Shock** | Pre-scripted Western Ghats + ICEGATE compound disruption | `POST /demo/dual-shock` |
| **Autopilot** | Fully automated 5-minute demo cycle with scripted narration | Sequential API calls |
| **Reset** | Restores all nodes/routes to baseline | State reset |


## 14.3 Autonomous Demo Mode (Autopilot)


**File**: `frontend/src/App.jsx` — `handleAutopilot()` callback


Scripted 5-minute autonomous demo:
1. Reset to baseline → verify ML Agent health
2. Inject Shock 1 (Monsoon) → wait for cascade visualization
3. Inject Shock 2 (ICEGATE) → trigger dual-shock rerouting
4. Display CascadeComparison modal with measured metrics
5. Final KPI summary
