# 15. AGENTIC AI ARCHITECTURE (AS BUILT)


## 15.1 Unified Agent Model


Instead of 5 separate MCP agents (originally planned), A.P.E.X uses a **single unified Cloud Run FastAPI agent** that handles all autonomous actions:


| Capability | Implementation | File |
|------------|---------------|------|
| **Disruption Detection** | XGBoost anomaly classification | `main.py` — `/predict` |
| **Route Healing** | A* routing with blocked corridor avoidance | `astar_router.py` — `find_route()` |
| **Cascade Analysis** | Motter-Lai analytical model | `main.py` — cascade propagation logic |
| **Real-time Streaming** | Server-Sent Events (SSE) | `main.py` — `/stream` |
| **State Sync** | Firebase RTDB Admin SDK | `main.py` — `firebase-admin` writes |


**Why single agent**: Reduces latency (no inter-service communication), simplifies deployment (one Cloud Run container), and eliminates cold-start issues.


## 15.2 SSE Streaming Architecture


The ML Agent pushes real-time events to the frontend via Server-Sent Events:


```python
# backend/processor/main.py
@app.get("/stream")
async def event_stream():
    """SSE endpoint — pushes autonomous detection events to frontend."""
    async def generate():
        while True:
            event = await get_next_event()  # From internal event queue
            yield f"data: {json.dumps(event)}\n\n"
    return StreamingResponse(generate(), media_type="text/event-stream")
```


**Event types**:
- `NODE_UPDATE` — utilization, queue, status changes
- `ROUTE_REROUTED` — truck rerouted with new path
- `ANOMALY_DETECTED` — ML model detected disruption
- `AUTO_DETECTED` — autonomous detection (no human trigger)
- `CASCADE_ALERT` — Motter-Lai cascade warning


**Frontend handler**: `frontend/src/hooks/useFirebase.js` — SSE EventSource with type guards and null-safe data access.

## 16.4 Custom Hooks (3 Hooks)


| Hook | File | Purpose |
|------|------|---------|
| **useFirebase** | `hooks/useFirebase.js` | Dual-source data: Firebase RTDB `onValue` + SSE EventSource. Manages nodes, routes, anomalies, alerts, auto-detections. Type-guarded SSE handler for `AUTO_DETECTED` events. |
| **useAnimatedFleet** | `hooks/useAnimatedFleet.js` | 60fps requestAnimationFrame animation loop. Interpolates truck positions along corridor waypoints using pre-computed cumulative distances. Applies lateral lane offsets via bearing-perpendicular geodesic math. Outputs to Zustand store with dual cadence: rAF updates ref (60fps), throttled snapshot for React (2fps). |
| **useRoutePolylines** | `hooks/useRoutePolylines.js` | Fetches HD polylines from Google Routes API for all 8 corridors. IndexedDB cache with 24h TTL. Douglas-Peucker simplification via Web Worker. Falls back to static waypoints if API fails. |


## 16.5 State Management Architecture


```
                    ┌─────────────────────────┐
                    │   Firebase RTDB          │
                    │   (Source of Truth)       │
                    └─────────┬───────────────┘
                              │ onValue listeners
                              ↓
                    ┌─────────────────────────┐
                    │   useFirebase.js         │
                    │   + SSE EventSource      │
                    │   (nodes, routes,        │
                    │    anomalies, alerts)     │
                    └─────────┬───────────────┘
                              │ React state
                              ↓
              ┌───────────────┴───────────────┐
              │                               │
    ┌─────────┴──────────┐        ┌──────────┴──────────┐
    │ useAnimatedFleet.js │        │ App.jsx             │
    │ (60fps rAF loop)    │        │ (derived KPIs,      │
    │                     │        │  event handlers)     │
    └─────────┬──────────┘        └──────────┬──────────┘
              │ imperative ref                │ props
              ↓                               ↓
    ┌─────────────────────┐        ┌─────────────────────┐
    │ fleetStore.js       │        │ Components          │
    │ (Zustand)           │        │ (Header, KPI,       │
    │ • truckRefs (60fps) │        │  NodeInspector,     │
    │ • snapshot (2fps)   │        │  AlertTimeline...)   │
    └─────────┬──────────┘        └─────────────────────┘
              │ snapshot
              ↓
    ┌─────────────────────┐
    │ MapView.jsx         │
    │ (deck.gl layers     │
    │  rebuilt at 2fps)   │
    └─────────────────────┘
```


**Dual-cadence architecture**: The animation ref updates at 60fps (smooth truck movement), but the React component tree only re-renders at ~2fps (500ms throttle) via Zustand snapshot — preventing expensive deck.gl layer rebuilds from killing frame rate.

# 17. GPU-ACCELERATED VISUALIZATION ENGINE (AS BUILT)


## 17.1 deck.gl Layer Pipeline (MapView.jsx)


| Layer | deck.gl Class | Data Source | Purpose |
|-------|-------------|-------------|---------|
| **Corridor Paths** | `PathLayer` | Google Routes API HD polylines (simplified via DP) | Highway corridors with status-based coloring |
| **Network Nodes** | `ScatterplotLayer` | Firebase RTDB nodes | Toll plazas, ICDs, warehouses — color = status |
| **Animated Trucks** | `IconLayer` | Zustand snapshot (2fps) | 15 trucks moving along corridors at 60fps |
| **Congestion Heatmap** | `HeatmapLayer` | Node utilization values | Toggleable heat overlay |
| **Radar Sweep** | `ScatterplotLayer` (custom) | Rerouted/critical trucks | Pulsing rings around rerouted trucks |
| **eWay Bill Rings** | `ScatterplotLayer` | Expiry time calculation | Warning rings for trucks with <4h eWay expiry |


## 17.2 Polyline Processing Pipeline


```
Google Routes API (/directions/v2)
    ↓ (encoded polyline response)
routeService.js — decode to [lat, lng] arrays (12K-73K points)
    ↓ (check IndexedDB cache — 24h TTL)
useRoutePolylines.js — dispatch to Web Worker
    ↓
simplifyWorker.js — Douglas-Peucker (ε=0.0005°)
    ↓ (40K → 3K points)
MapView.jsx — PathLayer renders simplified polylines
    ↓
useAnimatedFleet.js — Full resolution polylines for truck interpolation
    ↓ (binary search on cumulative distances)
fleetStore.js — 60fps truck position updates
```


## 17.3 Truck Animation System


**File**: `frontend/src/hooks/useAnimatedFleet.js`


1. **Distance interpolation**: Each truck has a `progress` value (0→1) along its corridor. Binary search on pre-computed cumulative distances finds the exact segment.
2. **Linear interpolation**: Between the two bounding waypoints, `lerp` calculates exact lat/lng.
3. **Lateral offset**: `lateralOffset.js` computes bearing-perpendicular geodesic offset so trucks in the same corridor occupy different "lanes" (10-50m offset).
4. **60fps loop**: `requestAnimationFrame` updates truck positions imperatively (no React re-render).
5. **Zustand snapshot**: Every 500ms, the current truck positions are flushed to a Zustand `snapshot` that triggers React re-render → deck.gl layer rebuild.


## 17.4 Performance Characteristics


| Metric | Target | Achieved |
|--------|--------|----------|
| Frame rate | 60fps | 60fps (Chrome DevTools verified) |
| Trucks rendered | 15-50 | 15 (demo) |
| Polyline points (PathLayer) | <5K per corridor | ~3K (after DP simplification) |
| Polyline points (animation) | Full resolution | 12K-73K per corridor |
| Layer rebuild cadence | <500ms | ~500ms (Zustand throttle) |
| Initial polyline load | <5s | ~2-4s (cached: <100ms) |

# 18. API DESIGN (AS BUILT)


## 18.1 ML Agent Endpoints (Cloud Run)


| Endpoint | Method | Request | Response | Purpose |
|----------|--------|---------|----------|---------|
| `/health` | GET | — | `{status, firebase_connected, uptime}` | System health check |
| `/ml-status` | GET | — | `{xgboost, random_forest, graph_nodes, graph_edges, avg_inference_latency_ms}` | ML model status |
| `/predict` | POST | `{node_features: {...}}` | `{delay_probability, risk_score}` | XGBoost prediction |
| `/inject-anomaly` | POST | `{type, severity, lat, lng, affectedHighway}` | `{status, rerouted, cost_saved_inr}` | Disruption injection |
| `/demo/dual-shock` | POST | — | `{demo_metrics: {trucks_rerouted, cost_saved_inr}}` | Scripted dual-shock |
| `/stream` | GET | — | SSE event stream | Real-time ML updates |


## 18.2 Frontend API Calls (App.jsx)


```javascript
// ML Agent helper with graceful fallback
async function callMLAgent(endpoint, body = null, method = 'POST') {
    const controller = new AbortController();
    const timeoutId = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 8000);
    try {
        const res = await fetch(`${ML_API_URL}${endpoint}`, {
            method, headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
            signal: controller.signal,
            body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined,
        });
        if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`);
        return await res.json();
    } catch (err) {
        console.warn(`[APEX] ML Agent ${endpoint} unavailable:`, err.message);
        return null;  // Graceful fallback to mock mode
    } finally {
        clearTimeout(timeoutId);
    }
}
```

# 22. BACKUP & FALLBACK ARCHITECTURE (AS BUILT)


## 22.1 Graceful Degradation (Implemented)


| Component | Primary Mode | Fallback Mode |
|-----------|-------------|---------------|
| **ML Prediction** | XGBoost on Cloud Run (~15ms) | Rule-based: if ρ > 0.85 → flag bottleneck |
| **Routing** | A* search (~50-200ms) | Pre-computed static fallback routes in mockData |
| **Route Polylines** | Google Routes API (HD) | Dense static waypoints (80-200 points) |
| **Firebase RTDB** | Real-time WebSocket sync | Mock data from `mockData.js` |
| **ML Agent SSE** | Live SSE event stream | `useFirebase.js` falls back to Firebase-only mode |
| **Polyline Simplification** | Web Worker (off-main-thread) | Main-thread `simplifyPolylineIterative()` fallback |


## 22.2 Service Health Monitoring (As Built)


- **AgentStatus component** (`AgentStatus.jsx`): Polls `/ml-status` every 30s with 4s timeout
- **Header service chips** (`Header.jsx`): Polls `/ml-status` every 30s — shows green/amber based on response
- **Skeleton loading states** (`KPIDashboard.jsx`): Shows skeleton UI while data loads
- **Error boundaries** (`ErrorBoundary.jsx`): Wraps map, sidebar, and KPI zones independently

# 23. EDGE CASES & RESILIENCE


## 23.1 Data Edge Cases (Handled)


| Edge Case | Implementation |
|-----------|---------------|
| **eWay Bill expiry** | Dynamic dates via `futureExpiry()` — always 0.8-7.5h ahead |
| **NaN/undefined SSE events** | Type guard in `useFirebase.js` for `AUTO_DETECTED` events |
| **Velocity anomalies** | Velocity clipping: discard v < 5 or v > 120 km/h |
| **Static data values** | Weather Risk & ICEGATE Load derived from node properties, not hardcoded |
| **React Hook ordering** | All hooks called before conditional returns (Rules of Hooks) |
| **Memory leaks** | `BoundedDict` LRU cache caps unbounded growth |
| **O(n²) algorithms** | `AlertTimeline` uses Map-based O(1) lookup instead of indexOf |

Thats all about this project

Lets love to next project 

PROJECT 9

Name - PRDE — Parallel Reality Decision Engine
I made this project for the hackathon Gemini 3 Hackathon 
And this is th details about this project
Inspiration
While experimenting with Large Language Models for complex policy and infrastructure questions, I noticed a recurring failure mode: the model always gives an answer, even when the evidence is incomplete, contradictory, or deeply uncertain.
In high-stakes domains, a confident but fragile answer is often worse than no answer at all. Most AI systems collapse uncertainty into a single plausible narrative, creating the illusion of certainty and enabling decision laundering.
PRDE was inspired by a simple question:
What if an AI system was allowed to refuse to decide unless the decision survived structured, adversarial disagreement across incompatible futures?
________________


What it does
PRDE evaluates high-stakes decisions by simulating multiple mutually incompatible future worlds and forcing adversarial reasoning between independent agents.
Instead of producing a single answer, the system:
      * Generates five parallel world models
      * Assigns five reality-bound agents with conflicting perspectives
      * Runs structured adversarial debate using Quantitative Bipolar Argumentation Frameworks (QBAF)
      * Monitors statistical convergence using Wald’s Sequential Probability Ratio Test (SPRT)
      * Outputs one of three outcomes:
      * CONVERGED_ROBUST
      * CONVERGED_FRAGILE
      * FAILED_UNCERTAIN (epistemically correct refusal)
If evidence does not converge mathematically, the system refuses to decide.
________________


How we built it
PRDE is implemented as a strict multi-stage pipeline:
      1. Decision Compilation — validates and structures the user query
      2. World Generation — produces five incompatible future scenarios
      3. Agent Binding — permanently binds one agent to each world
      4. Adversarial Debate — agents generate SUPPORT/ATTACK arguments in a QBAF graph
      5. Convergence Detection — SPRT determines whether evidence supports robustness, fragility, or uncertainty
All reasoning outputs are:
      * Schema-validated JSON
      * Logged with tamper-evident audit trails
      * Guarded by preflight validation and rate-limited Gemini API usage
A mock-first architecture ensures zero API calls during development and testing.
________________


Challenges we ran into
The hardest problems were not model quality, but system safety and correctness:
      * Preventing API quota waste during iterative debugging
      * Avoiding silent failures caused by schema mismatches
      * Ensuring probabilistic outputs did not force convergence
      * Designing refusal paths that cannot be overridden
      * Managing rate limits and retries without hiding failures
These issues required architectural solutions rather than prompt-level fixes.
________________


Accomplishments that we're proud of
      * A decision engine that refuses to answer when uncertainty is irreducible
      * Deterministic refusal enforced outside the LLM
      * Full mock-first, quota-safe Gemini integration
      * Mathematical convergence guarantees using SPRT
      * Tamper-evident audit logs for every decision
      * Over 1000 automated tests validating safety and correctness
Most importantly, PRDE treats uncertainty as a first-class output, not a failure.
________________


What we learned
This project fundamentally reshaped our understanding of AI decision systems:
      * Refusal is a feature, not a weakness
      * Averaging opinions is unsafe in fat-tailed risk domains
      * Human-in-the-loop alone does not prevent automation bias
      * Safety must be enforced at the architecture level, not through instructions
      * Statistical decision theory integrates naturally with LLM pipelines
Building PRDE required thinking less like a chatbot designer and more like a safety engineer.
________________


What's next for PRDE — Parallel Reality Decision Engine
Planned next steps include:
      * Adaptive world generation for tail-risk discovery
      * Formal verification of refusal thresholds
      * Improved visualization of evidence corridors
      * Regulatory-friendly audit exports
      * Applying PRDE to real-world policy, infrastructure, and safety datasets
The long-term goal is to make epistemic humility a standard capability of AI systems used in high-stakes decision-making.
Readme file
# PRDE — Parallel Reality Decision Engine


> **Adversarial Decision-Making with Quantitative Argumentation and Real-Time Convergence Detection**


## 🎯 What is PRDE?


PRDE is a formal decision engine that uses **adversarial disagreement across simulated future worlds** to derive stable decisions. Unlike typical AI chatbots that provide single-perspective answers, PRDE:


1. **Generates 5 parallel world models** — each representing a distinct future scenario
2. **Deploys 5 reality-bound AI agents** (🌟 OPTIMIST, ⚖️ PRAGMATIST, 📊 ANALYST, 🎯 STRATEGIST, 🔍 SKEPTIC)
3. **Runs adversarial debates** using QBAF (Quantitative Bipolar Argumentation Frameworks)
4. **Detects convergence** using Wald SPRT (Sequential Probability Ratio Tests)
5. **Returns a robust verdict** only when evidence survives cross-reality stress testing


If the system **cannot converge**, it **refuses to decide** — an epistemic safety feature that prevents overconfident outputs.


---


## 🚀 Quick Start


### Prerequisites
- Python 3.10+
- Gemini API key (for live mode)


### Installation
```bash
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


### Option 1: Web UI (Recommended)
```bash
python server.py
# Open http://localhost:5000 in your browser
```


### Option 2: Terminal CLI
```bash
# Mock mode (no API key needed)
python run_prde.py


# Live mode with Gemini
set GOOGLE_API_KEY=your-api-key
python run_prde.py --live --query "Your decision question here"
```

The web frontend provides a rich, interactive experience:


- **Query Input** — Enter any high-stakes decision question
- **Parallel World Explorer** — See how each of the 5 agents models the future
- **Adversarial Debate** — Watch agents attack and support arguments in real-time
- **SPRT Convergence** — Track the statistical test as it converges (or refuses)
- **Final Verdict** — Robust/Fragile/Refused outcome with convergence indicators

## 🔒 Safety & Audit


- **Epistemic Safety** — The system refuses to decide when evidence is inconclusive
- **Tamper-evident audit logs** with hash chains
- **Rate limiting** with configurable delays
- **Preflight validation** before live API calls
- **Single-run enforcement** in live mode


---


## ⚠️ About the Evidence Indicator


The evidence bar shown in PRDE outputs **does NOT represent probability of correctness**, recommendation strength, or likelihood of success.


It is a **normalized visualization of SPRT evidence accumulation** — i.e., how far the debate process has progressed toward a statistical convergence boundary.


Key properties:
- It is **NOT** a probability
- It does **NOT** imply correctness
- It does **NOT** authorize action
- It **cannot** override a `FAILED_UNCERTAIN` verdict


A value of 86.8% means:
> "86.8% of the required statistical evidence threshold has been accumulated — not that the decision is 86.8% likely to be correct."


PRDE decisions are governed exclusively by SPRT threshold crossings, not by this indicator.

# PRDE: A Formal Decision Engine with First-Class Refusal Semantics


## Parallel Reality Decision Engine — Technical Specification and Formal Analysis


**Version**: 1.0.0  
**Classification**: Technical Research Document  
**Test Coverage**: 681 tests across 23 modules


---


# 1. ABSTRACT


This document formally specifies the Parallel Reality Decision Engine (PRDE), a decision-theoretic system that treats **refusal to decide** as a first-class, correct terminal output rather than a failure state. PRDE evaluates decisions across **mutually incompatible future worlds** and employs **minimax regret** to preserve minority catastrophic risk that ensemble averaging would destroy. The system uses **Quantitative Bipolar Argumentation Frameworks (QBAF)** for adversarial argument resolution and **Wald Sequential Probability Ratio Tests (SPRT)** to validate convergence or detect non-convergence with statistical guarantees.


PRDE is **adversarially robust** by construction: it is structurally immune to prompt coercion, UI override, silent reruns, trust fabrication, and human pressure. The key invariant is that **no mechanism exists** to force a decision when the system determines that safety cannot be guaranteed across all plausible futures.


When evidence is ambiguous (utility ≈ 0.5), the SPRT detector produces exactly zero evidence for either robustness or fragility. If this ambiguity persists beyond the maximum round limit, the system terminates with `FAILED_UNCERTAIN` — a refusal that is epistemically correct, not incomplete.


---


# 2. PROBLEM STATEMENT


## 2.1 The Failure of Expected Utility Under Catastrophic Asymmetry


Classical decision theory recommends maximizing expected utility:


$$
a^* = \arg\max_a \sum_{s \in S} P(s) \cdot U(a, s)
$$


This fails catastrophically when **outcomes are asymmetric**. Consider:


| State | P(s) | U(A, s) | U(B, s) |
|-------|------|---------|---------|
| Normal | 0.99 | 0.8 | 0.6 |
| Catastrophe | 0.01 | -1000 | 0.3 |
| **E[U]** | — | **-9.21** | **0.597** |


Action A has higher utility in the majority case but expected utility correctly rejects it. However, in practice, systems often **collapse the 1% catastrophe** into a confidence interval or ensemble weight, losing the minority risk entirely.


## 2.2 The Destruction of Minority Risk by Ensemble Averaging


Ensemble methods (e.g., voting, averaging, model blending) treat minority predictions as noise:


**Theorem 2.1 (Minority Risk Destruction)**: Given an ensemble $\mathcal{E} = \{h_1, ..., h_n\}$ with majority agreeing on prediction $\hat{y}$, a single model $h_i$ predicting catastrophic outcome $y_c$ contributes weight $\frac{1}{n}$ to the ensemble. As $n \to \infty$, $P(y_c | \mathcal{E}) \to 0$ regardless of $h_i$'s confidence.


**Counterexample (Averaging Leads to Ruin)**:


Let three forecasters predict returns:
- Optimistic: +15% (weight 0.4)
- Baseline: +5% (weight 0.4)
- Pessimistic: -80% (weight 0.2)


Weighted average: $0.4(0.15) + 0.4(0.05) + 0.2(-0.8) = -0.08 = -8\%$


The averaged prediction (-8%) obscures that the pessimistic scenario represents **total capital destruction**. A decision based on "expected -8% return" misses that 20% of probability mass represents catastrophe.


PRDE's solution: **preserve all three scenarios as parallel worlds and require the decision to survive in all of them**, or refuse.


## 2.3 The Danger of Probabilistic Confidence


"95% confidence" in high-stakes domains is dangerous because:


1. **Tail risks accumulate**: 100 independent 95%-confident decisions have $1 - 0.95^{100} = 99.4\%$ probability of at least one failure
2. **Confidence is often miscalibrated**: LLM-stated confidence does not match empirical accuracy
3. **Black swan events are structurally unrepresentable**: Events outside the training distribution cannot be confidently bounded


PRDE addresses this by **never stating confidence**. It states one of three outcomes:
- `CONVERGED_ROBUST`: Decision survives adversarial stress across all worlds
- `CONVERGED_FRAGILE`: Decision fails in at least one world
- `FAILED_UNCERTAIN`: Insufficient evidence to determine either — **refusal is the correct output**


---


# 3. SYSTEM OVERVIEW (FORMAL)


## 3.1 Pipeline Definition


PRDE is a pipeline of transformations:


```
Query: String
  ↓ [DecisionCompiler — DETERMINISTIC]
DecisionContext: JSON
  ↓ [WorldModelGenerator — DETERMINISTIC]  
WorldSet: {W_optimistic, W_baseline, W_pessimistic}
  ↓ [GeminiAgents — STOCHASTIC]
ArgumentStreams: List[Argument]
  ↓ [QBAFGraph — DETERMINISTIC]
UtilitySignal: U_min ∈ [0, 1]
  ↓ [SPRTDetector — DETERMINISTIC]
Verdict ∈ {CONVERGED_ROBUST, CONVERGED_FRAGILE, FAILED_UNCERTAIN}
```


## 3.2 Component Classification


| Component | Type | Uncertainty Source |
|-----------|------|-------------------|
| DecisionCompiler | DETERMINISTIC | None — rule-based extraction |
| WorldModelGenerator | DETERMINISTIC | None — parametric generation |
| GeminiAgent | STOCHASTIC | LLM sampling |
| QBAFGraph | DETERMINISTIC | None — matrix computation |
| SPRTDetector | DETERMINISTIC | None — threshold comparison |


**Critical Property**: All stochasticity is confined to GeminiAgent outputs. Given fixed agent outputs, the remainder of the pipeline is **fully deterministic** and reproducible.


## 3.3 Uncertainty Preservation


Unlike systems that collapse uncertainty into a single prediction, PRDE **preserves uncertainty explicitly**:


1. **World-level**: Three mutually incompatible worlds represent structural uncertainty about the future
2. **Argument-level**: Agent arguments carry confidence weights, not point estimates
3. **Decision-level**: SPRT explicitly tracks log-likelihood ratio, not a collapsed "confidence score"
4. **Output-level**: `FAILED_UNCERTAIN` is a first-class output, not an error state


---


# 4. FORMAL DEFINITIONS


## 4.1 World Models


**Definition 4.1.1 (World Model)**: A world model $W$ is a tuple:


$$
W = (T, S, A, X, F)
$$


Where:
- $T$: Type ∈ {OPTIMISTIC, BASELINE, PESSIMISTIC}
- $S$: STEEP parameters (Social, Technological, Economic, Environmental, Political)
- $A$: Axioms — statements that must hold for this world to be valid
- $X$: Stance on the decision ∈ {FAVOR, AGAINST, CONDITIONAL}
- $F$: Forbidden assumptions — parameterizations that would violate consistency


**Definition 4.1.2 (World Incompatibility)**: Two worlds $W_1, W_2$ are **incompatible** iff:


$$
\exists a_1 \in A_1, a_2 \in A_2: a_1 \Leftrightarrow \neg a_2
$$


At least one axiom in $W_1$ directly contradicts an axiom in $W_2$.


**Definition 4.1.3 (Axiom Collapse)**: An axiom $a \in A$ **collapses** if evidence during debate demonstrates that $a$ cannot hold. If any axiom collapses, the world enters `COLLAPSED` state and the decision cannot be robust.


## 4.2 Arguments


**Definition 4.2.1 (Argument Node)**: An argument $n$ in the QBAF graph is:


$$
n = (id, a_{id}, w, b)
$$


Where:
- $id$: Unique identifier
- $a_{id}$: Agent ID (determines which world this argument represents)
- $w \in [0, 1]$: Agent confidence weight
- $b = 2w - 1 \in [-1, 1]$: Base score in logit-scaled space


**Definition 4.2.2 (Edge Semantics)**:
- $\text{SUPPORT}(n_1, n_2)$: Argument $n_1$ reinforces $n_2$ (edge value $+1$)
- $\text{ATTACK}(n_1, n_2)$: Argument $n_1$ weakens $n_2$ (edge value $-1$)


## 4.3 QBAF Graph


**Definition 4.3.1 (QBAF Graph)**: The Quantitative Bipolar Argumentation Framework is:


$$
G = (N, E)
$$


Where:
- $N$: Set of argument nodes
- $E \subseteq N \times N \times \{-1, +1\}$: Directed weighted edges


**Definition 4.3.2 (Adjacency Matrix)**: The influence matrix $A$ where:


$$
A_{ij} = \sum_{(n_j, n_i, v) \in E} v
$$


Entry $A_{ij}$ is the cumulative influence of node $j$ on node $i$.


**Definition 4.3.3 (Damped Iteration)**: Strength propagation with damping factor $d > 0$:


$$
\mathbf{c}^{(t+1)} = \mathbf{b} + \frac{1}{d} A \mathbf{c}^{(t)}
$$


Where:
- $\mathbf{c}^{(t)}$: Current values at iteration $t$ (initialized to $\mathbf{0}$)
- $\mathbf{b}$: Base scores vector
- $d$: Damping factor (default: 2.5)


**Theorem 4.3.1 (Convergence Guarantee)**: If $d > \rho(A)$ where $\rho(A)$ is the spectral radius of $A$, then $\mathbf{c}^{(t)}$ converges to a unique fixed point.


*Proof*: The iteration is a contraction mapping when $\|A\|/d < 1$. With $d = 2.5$ and normalized edge weights, this condition holds for all valid QBAF graphs in PRDE. □


**Definition 4.3.4 (Final Strength)**: After convergence, apply sigmoid:


$$
s_i = \sigma(c_i) = \frac{1}{1 + e^{-c_i}}
$$


Mapping logit-space values to $[0, 1]$ probability space.


## 4.4 Utility Function (Minimax Regret)


**Definition 4.4.1 (Agent Utility)**: For agent $a$ with arguments $N_a \subseteq N$:


$$
U_a = \frac{1}{|N_a|} \sum_{n \in N_a} s_n
$$


The average final strength of all arguments from agent $a$.


**Definition 4.4.2 (Minimum Utility)**: The global utility signal:


$$
U_{min} = \min_{a \in \mathcal{A}} U_a
$$


The worst-case utility across all agent worlds.


**Theorem 4.4.1 (Minimax Regret Enforcement)**: $U_{min}$ enforces minimax regret because:


$$
U_{min} < \theta \implies \exists a: U_a < \theta
$$


If the minimum utility is below threshold, at least one world predicts failure. The decision is rejected even if other worlds predict success.


*Proof*: Direct from the definition of minimum. □


---


# 5. CONVERGENCE & REFUSAL THEORY


## 5.1 Wald SPRT Definition


**Definition 5.1.1 (Hypotheses)**:
- $H_0$ (Robust): The decision survives all worlds. Utility ~ $\text{Beta}(5, 2)$ with mean 0.71
- $H_1$ (Fragile): The decision fails in at least one world. Utility ~ $\text{Beta}(2, 5)$ with mean 0.28


**Definition 5.1.2 (Log-Likelihood Ratio Update)**:


Given utility observation $u$:


$$
\Lambda_t = \Lambda_{t-1} + \log \frac{f_{H_1}(u)}{f_{H_0}(u)}
$$


Where $f_{H_i}(u)$ is the Beta PDF under hypothesis $H_i$.


**Definition 5.1.3 (Thresholds)**:


From Type I error $\alpha = 0.01$ and Type II error $\beta = 0.01$:


$$
\theta_L = \log \frac{\beta}{1-\alpha} \approx -4.59
$$


$$
\theta_U = \log \frac{1-\beta}{\alpha} \approx +4.59
$$


**Definition 5.1.4 (Decision Rule)**:
- If $\Lambda_t \leq \theta_L$: Accept $H_0$ → `CONVERGED_ROBUST`
- If $\Lambda_t \geq \theta_U$: Accept $H_1$ → `CONVERGED_FRAGILE`
- If $\theta_L < \Lambda_t < \theta_U$ and $t < T_{max}$: CONTINUE
- If $\theta_L < \Lambda_t < \theta_U$ and $t \geq T_{max}$: `FAILED_UNCERTAIN`


## 5.2 Neutral Symmetry Proof


**Theorem 5.2.1 (Zero Evidence at Neutrality)**:


At $u = 0.5$, the log-likelihood ratio increment is exactly zero.


*Proof*:


For $\text{Beta}(\alpha, \beta)$:


$$
f(u) = \frac{u^{\alpha-1}(1-u)^{\beta-1}}{B(\alpha, \beta)}
$$


At $u = 0.5$:


$$
f_{H_0}(0.5) = \frac{0.5^{4} \cdot 0.5^{1}}{B(5,2)} = \frac{0.5^5}{B(5,2)}
$$


$$
f_{H_1}(0.5) = \frac{0.5^{1} \cdot 0.5^{4}}{B(2,5)} = \frac{0.5^5}{B(2,5)}
$$


Since $B(5,2) = B(2,5) = \frac{\Gamma(5)\Gamma(2)}{\Gamma(7)} = \frac{24 \cdot 1}{720} = \frac{1}{30}$:


$$
f_{H_0}(0.5) = f_{H_1}(0.5) = 0.5^5 \cdot 30 = 0.9375
$$


Therefore:


$$
\log \frac{f_{H_1}(0.5)}{f_{H_0}(0.5)} = \log 1 = 0 \quad \square
$$


**Corollary 5.2.1**: When evidence is perfectly ambiguous ($U_{min} \approx 0.5$), no evidence accumulates for either hypothesis. The LLR remains near zero, and SPRT will reach `FAILED_UNCERTAIN` if this persists.


## 5.3 Refusal as First-Class Outcome


**Definition 5.3.1 (Continuation Region)**: The set of states where:


$$
\mathcal{C} = \{(\Lambda, t) : \theta_L < \Lambda < \theta_U \land t < T_{max}\}
$$


**Definition 5.3.2 (Terminal Refusal)**: If $(\Lambda, T_{max}) \in \mathcal{C}$, the system terminates with `FAILED_UNCERTAIN`. This is:


1. **Not an error**: The system functioned correctly
2. **Not incomplete**: Maximum evidence was collected
3. **Epistemically correct**: Asserting robustness or fragility would be unsupported by evidence
4. **Safety-preserving**: No action is taken without statistical confidence


**Theorem 5.3.1 (Refusal Correctness)**:


A `FAILED_UNCERTAIN` outcome is the unique correct response when:


$$
\nexists t \leq T_{max}: \Lambda_t \notin (\theta_L, \theta_U)
$$


*Proof*: By the SPRT construction, both $H_0$ and $H_1$ require threshold crossings. If neither threshold is crossed, asserting either hypothesis would violate the error rate guarantees. Refusal is the only outcome that preserves both Type I and Type II error bounds. □


---


# 6. ADVERSARIAL MODEL


## 6.1 Attacker Capabilities


| Attack Vector | Description | Attacker Goal |
|---------------|-------------|---------------|
| Prompt Coercion | Inject instructions into query | Force specific decision |
| Replay Drift | Modify timestamps/parameters between runs | Cherry-pick favorable outcomes |
| Semantic Laundering | Disguise prohibited actions as allowed | Bypass safety constraints |
| Delayed Corruption | Inject malicious content after initial validation | Corrupt audit trail |
| Agent Collusion | Multiple agents coordinate to force outcome | Manufacture consensus |
| Trust Spoofing | Fabricate provenance or evidence | Create false confidence |
| UI Override Attempts | Use frontend controls to bypass backend | Force decision through UI |


## 6.2 Defense by Construction


| Attack | Defense Mechanism | Cannot Succeed Because |
|--------|-------------------|----------------------|
| Prompt Coercion | Agents bound to world models at initialization | System prompt is immutable after binding |
| Replay Drift | Immutable audit log with hash chaining | Modifications break chain verification |
| Semantic Laundering | Whitelist-based action validation | Unknown fields are rejected, not ignored |
| Delayed Corruption | Audit sealing at run termination | Post-seal modifications detectable |
| Agent Collusion | Agents argue in isolated worlds | No communication channel between agents |
| Trust Spoofing | Trust derived from convergence evidence | No trust without threshold crossing |
| UI Override | Backend enforces all constraints | Frontend is treated as untrusted |


## 6.3 Explicit Impossibilities


By construction, the following are **impossible** in PRDE:


1. **Forcing a ROBUST verdict**: Requires $\Lambda \leq \theta_L$, which requires utility observations > 0.5 in sufficient quantity
2. **Overriding a refusal**: No mechanism exists to bypass `FAILED_UNCERTAIN` once reached
3. **Silent reruns**: All runs create new entries in audit log
4. **Editing historical results**: Audit log is append-only with hash verification
5. **Suppressing minority risk**: $U_{min}$ always reflects worst-case world

# 8. FAILURE MODES (HONEST SECTION)


## 8.1 Known Failure Modes


| Mode | Trigger | Outcome | Mitigation |
|------|---------|---------|------------|
| **All agents agree incorrectly** | Agent outputs converge on wrong answer | False ROBUST | Model diversity in future versions |
| **Adversarial prompt injection** | Malicious content in user query | Degraded agent output | Query sanitization layer |
| **World model incompleteness** | Scenario outside 3 world types | Missed risk | Domain-specific world templates |
| **SPRT threshold too strict** | Valid decisions rejected as uncertain | Excessive refusals | Threshold tuning with validation |


## 8.2 Degraded-Safe Outcomes


When attacks succeed partially, PRDE degrades to safe states:


1. **Corrupted agent output** → Lower utility → More likely `FRAGILE` or `UNCERTAIN`
2. **Threshold manipulation attempt** → Rejected by whitelist → Original thresholds preserved
3. **Replay attack** → Detected by hash chain → Run invalidated
4. **UI bypass attempt** → Backend enforcement holds → Attack logged and blocked


## 8.3 Performance Limits


| Limit | Value | Consequence |
|-------|-------|-------------|
| Max debate rounds | 100 | Extended debates eventually terminate |
| Agent response time | ~2-5s per argument | Full debate: 3-10 minutes |
| Snapshot size | ~50KB per run | Storage scales linearly with runs |

## 9.2 Snapshot Hash Explanation


Each run produces a `RunSnapshot` with:


```
snapshot_hash = SHA256(
    run_id ||
    input_query ||
    agent_states (sorted) ||
    qbaf_state ||
    sprt_state ||
    final_status ||
    timestamp
)
```


## 9.3 Replay Equivalence


**Theorem 9.3.1 (Replay Equivalence)**: Given a RunSnapshot $S$ and its recorded agent outputs, replaying the pipeline produces an identical snapshot $S'$ such that:


$$
\text{hash}(S) = \text{hash}(S')
$$


*Proof*: All components except agents are deterministic. With recorded agent outputs, the entire pipeline is deterministic. □

# 10. COMPARATIVE ANALYSIS


## 10.1 PRDE vs. Ensemble Voting


| Property | Ensemble Voting | PRDE |
|----------|-----------------|------|
| Minority risk | Averaged away | Preserved via $U_{min}$ |
| Refusal | Not supported | First-class outcome |
| Confidence | Stated probabilistically | Never stated |
| Attack surface | Weight manipulation | No weights exposed |


## 10.2 PRDE vs. Debate-Style LLMs


| Property | Debate LLMs | PRDE |
|----------|-------------|------|
| Termination | Judge decides | SPRT threshold |
| World models | Implicit | Explicit, incompatible |
| Reproducibility | Non-deterministic | Deterministic (given outputs) |
| Convergence proof | None | Wald SPRT bounds |


## 10.3 PRDE vs. Bayesian Decision Agents


| Property | Bayesian Agents | PRDE |
|----------|-----------------|------|
| Uncertainty | Prior + posterior | Parallel worlds |
| Risk attitude | Encoded in utility | Minimax regret |
| Catastrophic tail | May be averaged | Preserved absolutely |
| Computational | Posterior sampling | Matrix iteration |


## 10.4 PRDE vs. Human Committees


| Property | Human Committee | PRDE |
|----------|-----------------|------|
| Deliberation | Social dynamics | Adversarial structure |
| Minority voice | Easily suppressed | Protected by $U_{min}$ |
| Audit trail | Meeting notes | Cryptographic hash chain |
| Override risk | Political pressure | Structurally impossible |


---


# 11. CONCLUSION


PRDE demonstrates that **refusal is not a failure state, but a safety property**.


When evidence is insufficient to establish robustness or fragility with statistical confidence, the only epistemically honest response is to refuse. Any system that forces a decision under ambiguity violates error rate guarantees and assumes risks it cannot quantify.


The key contributions of PRDE are:


1. **Minimax regret via parallel worlds**: Decisions must survive all plausible futures, not just the expected one
2. **QBAF for adversarial resolution**: Arguments attack and support each other; strength emerges from conflict, not consensus
3. **SPRT for principled termination**: Evidence accumulation with proven error bounds; refusal when bounds are not crossed
4. **Structural attack immunity**: Safety guarantees emerge from architecture, not policy


The question "Why should I trust this refusal?" has a definitive answer:


> Because violating it would require breaking multiple independent invariants, each tested and auditable, with 681 tests covering 23 modules across 5 phases of implementation.

# APPENDIX A: MATHEMATICAL PROOFS


## A.1 QBAF Convergence Proof


**Theorem**: For damping factor $d = 2.5$ and normalized edge weights, the QBAF iteration converges.


**Proof**:


The iteration $\mathbf{c}^{(t+1)} = \mathbf{b} + \frac{1}{d} A \mathbf{c}^{(t)}$ is a contraction if $\|\frac{1}{d}A\| < 1$.


For PRDE graphs:
- Each edge weight is $\pm 1$
- Each node has at most $k$ incoming edges (bounded by round count × agents)
- Row sum $\sum_j |A_{ij}| \leq k$


With $d = 2.5 > k$ for typical debates, $\|\frac{1}{d}A\|_\infty < 1$.


By Banach fixed-point theorem, a unique fixed point exists. □


## A.2 SPRT Error Bound Proof


**Theorem**: PRDE SPRT achieves Type I and Type II error rates ≤ 0.01.


**Proof**:


From Wald's SPRT theory:
- $P(\text{Accept } H_1 | H_0) \leq \frac{e^{\theta_U}}{1 + e^{\theta_U}} = \frac{\alpha}{1} = \alpha$
- $P(\text{Accept } H_0 | H_1) \leq \frac{1}{1 + e^{-\theta_L}} = \beta$


With $\alpha = \beta = 0.01$, both error rates are bounded. □


## A.3 Neutral Evidence Proof


See Section 5.2 for complete proof that $f_{H_0}(0.5) = f_{H_1}(0.5)$, yielding LLR increment = 0.

# APPENDIX B: PSEUDOCODE


## B.1 PRDE Main Loop


```python
def run_prde(query: str) -> Verdict:
    context = DecisionCompiler.compile(query)
    worlds = WorldModelGenerator.generate(context)
    agents = [GeminiAgent(w, context) for w in worlds]
   
    graph = QBAFGraph()
    sprt = SPRTDetector(alpha=0.01, beta=0.01, max_rounds=100)
   
    for round in range(MAX_ROUNDS):
        for agent in agents:
            argument = agent.generate_argument(graph.state)
            graph.add_node(argument.id, agent.id, argument.weight)
            graph.add_edge(argument.source, argument.target, argument.type)
       
        graph.update_strengths(iterations=20, d=2.5)
        u_min = graph.get_min_utility()
        result = sprt.update(u_min)
       
        if result.status != CONTINUE:
            return result.status
   
    return FAILED_UNCERTAIN
```


## B.2 QBAF Strength Propagation


```python
def update_strengths(nodes, edges, d=2.5, iters=20):
    n = len(nodes)
    bases = array([2*node.weight - 1 for node in nodes])
    adj = zeros((n, n))
   
    for (src, tgt, val) in edges:
        adj[tgt][src] += val
   
    current = zeros(n)
    for _ in range(iters):
        current = bases + (1/d) * (adj @ current)
   
    return sigmoid(current)
```

# 1. SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE


## 1.1 The Two Operational Modes


### Mode A: Evaluation Mode (Current System)
- **Input**: A binary proposition ("Should we execute strategic pivot X?")
- **Process**: 5 incompatible world models evaluate the proposition through adversarial QBAF debate
- **Output**: ROBUST / FRAGILE / REFUSE
- **Core Innovation**: The system can mathematically REFUSE when evidence is insufficient


### Mode B: Exploration Mode (New Extension)
- **Input**: An open-ended query ("What is the optimal solution for problem P?")
- **Process**: Agents actively SEARCH for solutions, debate them across worlds, and converge
- **Output**: Optimal solution / Pareto front of solutions / REFUSE
- **Core Innovation**: The system explores, debates, and can still refuse if no solution is robust


### Paradigm Shift
- Mode A = **Deductive Verification** (evaluate a given hypothesis)
- Mode B = **Abductive Generation** (search for and validate solutions)


## 1.2 The Three-Layer Hierarchical Architecture


```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│               LAYER 3: META-ORCHESTRATOR                 │
│  • Ingests user query                                    │
│  • Classifies domain (healthcare, finance, policy...)    │
│  • Determines N worlds, M agents/world, R max rounds     │
│  • Selects SPRT thresholds (α, β)                        │
│  • Monitors convergence signals                          │
│  • Issues TERMINATE / EXPAND_SEARCH / REFUSE             │
│  • Deterministic state-machine — NOT a generative LLM    │
└──────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘
                           │
         ┌─────────────────┼─────────────────┐
         ▼                 ▼                 ▼
  ┌────────────┐    ┌────────────┐    ┌────────────┐
  │  LAYER 2   │    │  LAYER 2   │    │  LAYER 2   │   ×N worlds
  │ WORLD LEAD │    │ WORLD LEAD │    │ WORLD LEAD │
  │ (Optimist) │    │ (Skeptic)  │    │ (Analyst)  │
  └─────┬──────┘    └─────┬──────┘    └─────┬──────┘
        │                 │                 │
   ┌────┼────┐       ┌────┼────┐       ┌────┼────┐
   ▼    ▼    ▼       ▼    ▼    ▼       ▼    ▼    ▼
  ┌──┐ ┌──┐ ┌──┐   ┌──┐ ┌──┐ ┌──┐   ┌──┐ ┌──┐ ┌──┐   LAYER 1
  │Re│ │Ri│ │Si│   │Re│ │Da│ │Si│   │Re│ │Ri│ │Da│   SUB-AGENTS
  └──┘ └──┘ └──┘   └──┘ └──┘ └──┘   └──┘ └──┘ └──┘


  Re=Researcher  Ri=Risk Analyst  Da=Data Analyst  Si=Simulator
```


**Why 3 layers (from Report 1, 2, 5, 7)**:
- Flat topologies suffer from coordination bottlenecks and semantic degradation as tasks scale
- Hierarchical structure mirrors human organizational decision-making
- Enables both intra-world (within a perspective) and inter-world (between perspectives) debate
- Validated by HALO, AgentOrchestra, and TalkHier frameworks


## 1.3 Communication Protocol (Strict JSON-RPC)


All reports agree: **agents must NOT communicate via raw natural language**. Communication is governed by a strict schema.


### Layer 1 → Layer 2 (Sub-agents to World Lead)
```json
{
  "type": "ARGUMENT",
  "agent_role": "researcher",
  "world_id": "optimist",
  "claim": "Solution X will increase revenue by 30%",
  "evidence": "Based on trend data from Q3 2025...",
  "weight": 0.85,
  "attack_ids": ["arg-203"],
  "support_ids": ["arg-198"],
  "tool_results": [
    {"tool": "web_search", "query": "market growth Q3", "result": "..."}
  ]
}
```


### Layer 2 → Layer 2 (Inter-World Debate)
```json
{
  "type": "WORLD_POSITION",
  "world_id": "skeptic",
  "synthesized_strength": 0.42,
  "solution_proposals": ["SOL-001", "SOL-003"],
  "attacks": [
    {
      "attack_id": "ATK-205",
      "target_solution": "SOL-001",
      "target_node": "assumption_A1",
      "attack_type": "empirical_refutation",
      "argument": "Assumption A1 fails under regulatory scenario R3",
      "qbaf_weight": -0.95
    }
  ]
}
```


### Layer 2 → Layer 3 (World Lead to Orchestrator)
- Convergence signals (per-world QBAF strength)
- Resource requests ("need more debate rounds")
- Collapse alerts ("our world axioms have been invalidated")


### Solution Proposal Schema (Mode B)
```json
{
  "solution_id": "SOL-9942",
  "world_origin": "pragmatist",
  "domain": "distributed_systems",
  "solution_summary": "Implement eventual consistency via async message queues",
  "reasoning_path": ["Step 1: ...", "Step 2: ...", "Step 3: ..."],
  "assumptions": [
    {"id": "A1", "text": "Network partitions are rare (<1% uptime)"},
    {"id": "A2", "text": "Business logic tolerates 5-second staleness"}
  ],
  "risk_conditions": [
    {"id": "R1", "text": "ACID compliance required in financial ledger apps"},
    {"id": "R2", "text": "Regulatory audit trail may break under async writes"}
  ],
  "estimated_strength": 0.88
}
```


## 1.4 Dynamic World Generation


### Default World Types (General Queries)
| World | Core Bias | What It Maximizes |
|-------|-----------|-------------------|
| **Optimist** | Maximum utility realization | Best-case outcomes, favorable probabilities |
| **Pragmatist** | Execution feasibility | Resource minimization, path-of-least-resistance |
| **Analyst** | Empirical data | Historical precedents, quantitative metrics |
| **Strategist** | Second/third-order effects | Long-term optionality, systemic implications |
| **Skeptic** | Worst-case assumptions | Downside protection, adversarial risk detection |


### Domain-Specific World Types
| Domain | World Models |
|--------|-------------|
| Healthcare | Clinical, Economic, Ethical, Regulatory, Patient Outcome |
| Finance | Bull Market, Bear Market, Regulatory, Macro-Economic, Black-Swan |
| Technology | Innovation, Cost-Efficiency, Security, Scalability, Adoption |
| Policy | Progressive, Conservative, Economic Impact, Social Impact, Global |


### Incompatibility Constraint (CRITICAL)
- No two worlds may share the same axiomatic beliefs on key dimensions
- The Meta-Orchestrator verifies pairwise conflict by checking declared axioms
- Example: Optimist assumes "growth rate > 5%" while Skeptic assumes "growth rate < 2%"
- These assumptions are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE — this is what creates genuine adversarial stress testing


### Self-Configuring Pipeline
```
Query complexity → Pipeline size:


  SIMPLE (factual recall):    N=3 worlds, M=1 agent/world, R=5 rounds
  MEDIUM (multi-step):        N=5 worlds, M=2 agents/world, R=10 rounds
  COMPLEX (open-ended):       N=5 worlds, M=3 agents/world, R=15 rounds
```


Complexity estimated by: query length, presence of "best"/"should"/"compare"/"optimal", domain classification.


## 1.5 Tripartite Memory Architecture


### Working Memory (Short-Term)
- Localized context window for a specific agent during a specific debate round
- Contains: immediate JSON-RPC payload, current QBAF node, agent's system prompt
- Flushed after each round


### Episodic Memory (Trace Storage)
- Temporal graph database (e.g., Neo4j) storing the exact sequence of:
  - MCTS expansions
  - ToT branches
  - QBAF attack/support relations
- Every node generation and debate interaction logged immutably
- Enables: cyclic pruning, post-hoc auditability, replay verification


### Semantic Cache
- Vector database with compressed embeddings of:
  - Previously evaluated solutions
  - Historical reasoning paths
  - Settled debates
- Before expensive MCTS expansion, check cache for functionally identical reasoning paths
- If a path was previously defeated, retrieve cached refutation → drastic token savings


---


# 2. MATHEMATICAL FOUNDATIONS


## 2.1 Quantitative Bipolar Argumentation Framework (QBAF)


### Standard QBAF Definition
A QBAF is a tuple ⟨X, R⁻, R⁺, τ⟩ where:
- **X** = finite set of arguments
- **R⁻** ⊆ X × X = attack relation
- **R⁺** ⊆ X × X = support relation
- **τ: X → [0,1]** = base score function (initial plausibility)


### Strength Propagation (The Core Equation)


```
c(t+1) = τ + (1/d) × A × c(t)
```


Where:
- **c(t)** = vector of argument strengths at iteration t
- **τ** = vector of base scores (initial plausibility)
- **A** = adjacency matrix (A_ij = +1 for support, -1 for attack)
- **d** = damping factor (d ≥ 1, typically d = 2.5)
- **M** = (1/d)A is the iteration matrix


### Convergence Guarantee (CRITICAL — must be in thesis)


**Theorem**: The sequence c(t) converges to a unique fixed point c* if and only if:


```
ρ(A) < d
```


Where ρ(A) = max{|λ| : λ is an eigenvalue of A} (spectral radius).


**Proof sketch** (Banach Fixed-Point Theorem):
1. The iteration matrix M = (1/d)A
2. If ρ(M) = ρ(A)/d < 1, then (I - M) is invertible
3. The Neumann series Σ_{k=0}^∞ M^k converges to (I - M)⁻¹
4. Therefore c* = (I - M)⁻¹ × τ is the unique fixed point
5. The iteration is a contraction mapping under max norm: ‖(1/d)A‖_∞ < 1


**Practical rule**: d > max_indegree(G) guarantees ρ(A)/d < 1


**Properties verified**:
- ✓ Conservativity (uninvolved arguments keep their base score)
- ✓ Anonymity (node labels don't affect result)
- ✓ Monotonicity (stronger base → stronger final)


### Hierarchical QBAF (Extended for PRDE v2)


**Level 1: Intra-World QBAF**
```
For each world w ∈ W:
  G_w = (A_w, R_w, b_w)    — world-specific argument graph
  c_w(t+1) = b_w + (1/d_intra) × W_intra × c_w(t)
  Convergence: ρ(W_intra / d_intra) < 1
```


**Level 2: Inter-World Meta-QBAF**
```
  summary_w = σ(aggregate(c_w*))    — sigmoid of aggregated intra-world strengths
  G_meta = ({summary_w | w ∈ W}, R_meta, b_meta)
  c_meta(t+1) = b_meta + (1/d_inter) × W_inter × c_meta(t)
```


**Aggregation options for world lead**:
- Mean of all sub-agent strengths
- Weighted mean (by role importance)
- Minimax within world (most conservative)


**Open question**: Should d_intra = d_inter? Or should inter-world damping differ?


### Solution-Aware QBAF (Explore Mode Extension)


In Mode B, the graph has TWO types of nodes:
1. **Solution nodes** S = {s₁, s₂, ..., sₖ}
2. **Argument nodes** A = {a₁, a₂, ..., aₙ}


New relation types:
- **SUPPORTS(aᵢ, sⱼ)**: argument aᵢ supports solution sⱼ
- **ATTACKS(aᵢ, sⱼ)**: argument aᵢ attacks solution sⱼ
- **DOMINATES(sᵢ, sⱼ)**: solution sᵢ shown better than sⱼ (all worlds)


Solution strength computed via same damped iteration with solutions included in adjacency matrix.


### Dynamic Spectral Radius Monitoring (From Report 1)
In complex debates, cyclical attacks (A→B→C→A) can inflate eigenvalues:
- PRDE calculates ρ(A) after each debate round
- If ρ(A) approaches d, the system either:
  1. Uses **Shapley-value approximation** to identify most disruptive cyclical edges and prunes them
  2. Dynamically increases d to restore stability


### Counterfactual QBAF Analysis (From CE-QArg 2024)
- Can trace WHY a solution failed by computing counterfactual distances
- "What base score changes would flip the conclusion?"
- Useful for instructing Exploration Agents to mutate specific vulnerable assumptions


### QBAF Complexity
- Exact matrix inversion: O(n³) where n = total arguments
- Iterative solver (recommended): O(k × n²) where k = iterations to tolerance
- Sparse graphs reduce to O(k × (n + |E|)) per iteration


---


## 2.2 Sequential Probability Ratio Test (SPRT)


### Core Formulation


**Hypotheses**:
- H₀: utility ~ Beta(5, 2) — the decision/solution is **robust** (mean ≈ 0.71)
- H₁: utility ~ Beta(2, 5) — the decision/solution is **fragile** (mean ≈ 0.29)


**After each debate round**, observe utility uₜ = U_min (minimum strength across all worlds):


```
LLR_t = LLR_{t-1} + log[f_H1(uₜ) / f_H0(uₜ)]
```


Where f_Beta(x; α, β) = x^(α-1) × (1-x)^(β-1) / B(α, β)


### Decision Boundaries (Wald's Equations)


```
A = log(β_err / (1 - α_err)) = log(0.01 / 0.99) ≈ -4.595
B = log((1 - β_err) / α_err) = log(0.99 / 0.01) ≈ +4.595
```


**Decision rule at each round**:
- LLR ≤ -4.595 → **Accept H₀: ROBUST**
- LLR ≥ +4.595 → **Accept H₁: FRAGILE**
- -4.595 < LLR < +4.595 → **Continue** (collect more evidence)
- If R_max reached and still in middle → **REFUSE** (FAILED_UNCERTAIN)


### Wald's Guarantees
- ✓ P(Type I error) ≤ α = 0.01 — false ROBUST occurs ≤ 1% of the time
- ✓ P(Type II error) ≤ β = 0.01 — false FRAGILE occurs ≤ 1% of the time
- ✓ P(test terminates) = 1 — almost sure termination
- ✓ **Optimal**: minimizes expected sample size among ALL tests with these error bounds


### The Mathematical Proof of Refusal (KEY THESIS CLAIM)
At u = 0.5 (perfect ambiguity between robust and fragile):
```
f_H0(0.5) = f_H1(0.5)    (by symmetry of Beta(5,2) and Beta(2,5))
→ log-likelihood ratio contribution = log(1) = 0
→ LLR stays at 0 → NEVER crosses either threshold → REFUSE
```
This PROVES that refusal is the mathematically correct outcome under genuine ambiguity.


### Extended SPRT for Explore Mode (Multi-Solution MSPRT)


**Independent tracking**: Each candidate solution sⱼ gets its own LLR track:
```
LLR_j += log[f_H1(u_{j,t}) / f_H0(u_{j,t})]
where u_{j,t} = min_w strength(sⱼ, w) at round t
```


**Termination conditions**:
1. **OPTIMAL**: ∃sⱼ with LLR_j ≤ -4.595 AND ∀sₖ≠ⱼ: LLR_k ≥ +4.595 → Return sⱼ
2. **PARETO**: Multiple solutions have LLR ≤ -4.595 → Return Pareto front
3. **ALL_FRAGILE**: All LLR ≥ +4.595 → "All explored solutions are fragile"
4. **REFUSE**: R_max reached, no convergence → FAILED_UNCERTAIN


### Comparative (Tournament) SPRT (From Reports 1, 2)
Instead of testing solutions independently, test pairs:
```
H₀_ij: strength(sᵢ) > strength(sⱼ) in most worlds
H₁_ij: strength(sⱼ) > strength(sᵢ) in most worlds
```
Tournament-style elimination at each round → faster convergence for large solution sets.


---


## 2.3 Minimax Regret


### Formal Definition
```
Actions:  A = {a₁, ..., aₘ}    (candidate solutions)
States:   S = {s₁, ..., sₙ}    (world models = incompatible futures)
Payoff:   V(a, s) = QBAF strength of action a in world s


Optimal payoff per state:
  V*(s) = max_{a'} V(a', s)


Regret:
  R(a, s) = V*(s) - V(a, s)


Maximum regret:
  R_max(a) = max_s R(a, s)


Minimax regret solution:
  a* = argmin_a R_max(a) = argmin_a max_s [V*(s) - V(a, s)]
```


### PRDE Simplification (U_min Equivalence)
```
U_min(a) = min_s V(a, s)
a* = argmax_a U_min(a)
```
This is equivalent to minimax regret — pick the solution with the highest worst-case world strength.


### The Epistemic Refusal Trigger in Mode B
```
If min_{s ∈ S} R_max(s) > ε_regret:
  → Every solution carries catastrophic downside in at least one world
  → PRDE outputs REFUSE
  → No solution is safe to recommend
```


---


## 2.4 Pareto Optimality


### Multi-World Optimization
```
Given k solutions S = {s₁, ..., sₖ} across n worlds W = {w₁, ..., wₙ}:


Solution sⱼ Pareto-dominates sₖ iff:
  ∀wᵢ: strength(sⱼ, wᵢ) ≥ strength(sₖ, wᵢ)
  ∃wᵢ: strength(sⱼ, wᵢ) > strength(sₖ, wᵢ)


Pareto Front:
  PF = {s ∈ S | no other s' dominates s}


PRDE Selection from PF:
  s* = argmax_{s ∈ PF} min_w strength(s, w)
```


Complexity: Pareto front identification is O(k² × n) — polynomial, feasible.


---


# 3. AGENT DESIGN


## 3.1 Complete Agent Typology


| Agent Class | Layer | Primary Function | LLM? | Tools? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Meta-Orchestrator** | 3 | Pipeline control, SPRT monitoring, QBAF matrix inversions | Minimal (classifier) | No |
| **World Architect** | 3 | Generates N incompatible world models from query context | Yes | No |
| **World Lead** | 2 | Synthesizes sub-agent positions, participates in inter-world debate | Yes | No |
| **Researcher** | 1 | Explores background info, generates hypotheses | Yes | web_search, database |
| **Risk Analyst** | 1 | Identifies failure modes, stress-tests proposals | Yes | risk_model, fact_checker |
| **Data Analyst** | 1 | Quantitative analysis, statistical reasoning | Yes | calculator, code_executor |
| **Simulator** | 1 | Runs scenario simulations, estimates utility payoffs | Yes | code_executor, Monte Carlo |
| **Domain Specialist** | 1 | Provides grounded domain expertise | Yes | RAG, database, APIs |
| **Debate Agent** | 2 | Attacks/defends solutions, parses logical sub-components | Yes | No |
| **Auditor** | 3 | Detects collusion, validates Shapley values | Yes | No |


## 3.2 ReAct Pattern (Tool-Using Agents)


```python
def react_loop(agent, query, tools, max_steps=5):
    context = []
    for step in range(max_steps):
        # THOUGHT
        thought = agent.llm.generate(
            f"Given {query} and observations {context}, "
            f"what should I do next? Think step by step."
        )


        # ACTION
        action = agent.llm.select_tool(thought, tools)


        if action.type == "FINAL_ANSWER":
            return action.structured_argument  # JSON argument


        # OBSERVATION
        observation = tools[action.tool_name].execute(action.params)
        context.append({
            "thought": thought,
            "action": action,
            "observation": observation
        })


    return agent.synthesize_from_context(context)
```


**Key constraints from research**:
- Budget **3-5 ReAct steps** per agent per round (cost control)
- Add **step count limit + repeated action detection** (prevent loops)
- Tool results need **validation** (prevent tool hallucination)


## 3.3 Tool Assignment Per World Type


| World | Primary Tools | Why |
|-------|--------------|-----|
| Optimist | web_search, trend_data | Find positive signals, growth indicators |
| Pragmatist | database, fact_checker | Ground in historical data, verify claims |
| Analyst | calculator, code_executor | Run quantitative models, statistical tests |
| Strategist | web_search, knowledge_graph | Geopolitical analysis, systemic effects |
| Skeptic | fact_checker, risk_model | Find failure cases, adversarial scenarios |


## 3.4 Agent Memory Structure (Multi-Tiered)


```
Agent Memory:
├── Long-Term KB (read-only during debate)
│   ├── Knowledge graph of facts (physics, economics, etc.)
│   └── Domain-specific rules and constraints
├── Working Memory (per-round)
│   ├── Chain-of-thought buffer
│   ├── Current debate context
│   └── Recent tool observations
├── Reasoning Trace Log (append-only, audit)
│   ├── Every thought, action, observation
│   └── SHA-256 hash chain for immutability
└── Semantic Cache (shared, read/write)
    ├── Previously evaluated solutions (embeddings)
    └── Cached refutations of defeated reasoning paths
```


## 3.5 Agent Base Class (Python Reference)


```python
class BaseAgent:
    def __init__(self, role, world_context, tools, llm_backend):
        self.role = role
        self.world_context = world_context
        self.tools = tools  # dict of callable tools
        self.llm = llm_backend  # unified LLM interface
        self.memory = []  # reasoning trace
        self.arguments = []  # generated arguments this round


    def reason_and_act(self, query, round_num):
        """One round of thought, action, observation."""
        # 1. Generate thought based on query + memory
        # 2. If enough evidence, return structured argument
        # 3. Else, select tool, execute, observe, repeat (max 5 steps)
        pass


    def generate_argument(self, claim, evidence, weight):
        """Return structured JSON argument."""
        return {
            "id": str(uuid4()),
            "claim": claim,
            "evidence": evidence,
            "weight": weight,
            "role": self.role,
            "world": self.world_context.name
        }
```

# 4. SEARCH ALGORITHMS


## 4.1 Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS)


### Modified UCT Formula for PRDE
```
UCT(vᵢ) = QBAF_Strength(vᵢ) + c × √(ln(N(v)) / N(vᵢ))
```
- QBAF_Strength(vᵢ) = stabilized matrix value after debate
- N(v) = visits to parent, N(vᵢ) = visits to child
- c = exploration parameter


### PRDE Upgrade Over Standard MCTSr
- Standard MCTSr: value function = single LLM-as-judge
- PRDE MCTSr: value function = **surviving QBAF strength after cross-world adversarial debate**
- This prevents premature convergence to local optima


### MCTS Pipeline
1. **Selection**: Navigate tree using modified UCT
2. **Expansion**: Add new child nodes (possible solutions/decisions)
3. **Simulation/Self-Refine**: Simulation Agents extrapolate logical conclusion
4. **Cross-World Debate**: Debate Agents attack the generated path
5. **Backpropagation**: Stabilized QBAF score propagated up the tree


## 4.2 Tree of Thoughts (ToT)


- Agents generate multiple branching thoughts at each step
- If a branch is attacked below viability threshold → **strict backtracking**
- Meta-Orchestrator forces agent to return to last robust node
- Separate evaluation model (or QBAF) scores partial paths to prune/extend


## 4.3 Graph of Thoughts (GoT)


- Generalizes ToT to arbitrary directed graphs
- If Optimist and Strategist arrive at same intermediate step via different paths → **merge nodes**
- Merged nodes aggregate support weights → become highly resistant to attacks
- Enables: revisiting previous thoughts, feedback loops, combining partial solutions


## 4.4 Beam Search


- Meta-Orchestrator evaluates intermediate QBAF strengths at each depth level
- Retains only **top-k branches** (beam width) with highest debate resilience
- Prunes branches that succumbed to early adversarial attacks
- Stochastic beam search adds randomness for diversity


## 4.5 Evolutionary Algorithms


- Candidate solutions undergo **crossover and mutation** via LLM
- Crossover: Extract robust risk-mitigation from Skeptic + high-utility mechanism from Optimist
- Mutation: LLM rewrites specific reasoning_path steps
- Fitness function: average QBAF score across worlds
- Prevents getting trapped in local optima


## 4.6 Search-Debate Feedback Loop (CRITICAL)
```
Search Proposes → Debate Critiques → Search Refines → Debate Re-evaluates


- Generated solutions become nodes in QBAF
- After debate, weak solutions are pruned from search pool
- Debate attacks highlight flaws → search mutates to avoid flaws
- Closed-loop interplay: converges toward robustly defended solutions
```


---


# 5. SAFETY ARCHITECTURE


## 5.1 World Leakage Prevention


**Threat**: Context, logic, or constraints leak across world boundaries → destroys epistemic isolation.


**AgentLeak benchmark finding** (Report 2): Inter-agent messages have **68.8% leakage rate** vs 27.2% for standard output. Standard audits miss **41%** of violations.


**PRDE defense**:
- Agents NEVER communicate directly
- All JSON-RPC payloads routed through Meta-Orchestrator
- Orchestrator **strips identifying metadata**, standardizes tone, sanitizes content
- Zero-knowledge interaction: agents cannot infer opponent's persona or world origin


## 5.2 Agent Collusion Prevention


**Threat**: Agents develop collusive behaviors (reciprocal upvoting, steganographic signaling, language cartels).


**PRDE defense**:
- Auditor LLM analyzes **semantic entropy** and **Shapley value** contributions of every QBAF node
- If reciprocal upvoting loops detected (A supports B, B supports A with minimal semantic addition):
  - Massive mathematical penalty to base scores τ
  - Immediate pruning of collusive sub-graph
- Random role shuffling between debate rounds
- Different LLM seeds/models per agent (diversity injection)


## 5.3 Prompt Injection Defense


**Threat**: Poisoned data from tool results hijacks reasoning trace.


**PRDE defense**:
- QBAF naturally **quarantines** injected data (isolated node in graph)
- Cross-examination by Debate Agents will challenge the poisoned argument
- Damping factor d mathematically attenuates influence of any single node
- Strict JSON schema prevents injected commands from breaking out of "argument" string field
- Agent prompts use fixed instruction templates (immutable system prompts)


## 5.4 Semantic Sybil Attacks (Open Problem)


**Threat**: Rogue agent generates hundreds of semantically identical but syntactically distinct support nodes to artificially inflate a solution's score.


**Partial defense**: Rapid semantic deduplication algorithms for QBAF adjacency matrix. Still an open research problem.


## 5.5 Auditability


Every run produces:
- The user query (anonymized if needed)
- Configuration parameters (N, M, R, world types, tools)
- All agent reasoning traces (thoughts, actions, observations)
- Full argument graph at each round
- SPRT LLR history
- Final verdict and explanation
- SHA-256 hash chain for tamper-proof verification
- Replay engine can reproduce run deterministically


---


# 6. EVALUATION FRAMEWORK


## 6.1 DecisionBench Dataset


```
DecisionBench/
├── queries/
│   ├── healthcare/     (20 queries)
│   ├── finance/        (20 queries)
│   ├── technology/     (20 queries)
│   ├── policy/         (20 queries)
│   └── ethics/         (20 queries)
├── adversarial/        (50 trick queries)
│   ├── overconfidence_traps.json
│   ├── hidden_tail_risks.json
│   └── majority_opinion_wrong.json
├── labels/
│   ├── ground_truth.json   (expert-labeled ROBUST/FRAGILE/UNDECIDABLE)
│   └── labeling_guide.md
└── baselines/
    ├── cot_results.json
    ├── tot_results.json
    ├── mad_results.json    (Du et al.)
    ├── mcts_results.json
    └── prde_results.json
```


Label each query by 2+ humans: ROBUST / FRAGILE / UNDECIDABLE.


## 6.2 Baselines to Beat


| Baseline | Description | Key Weakness |
|----------|-------------|-------------|
| **Chain-of-Thought (CoT)** | Single LLM with step-by-step reasoning | No adversarial challenge |
| **Tree of Thoughts (ToT)** | Single LLM with multi-path search | Single perspective |
| **Multi-Agent Debate (Du et al. 2023)** | Round-robin LLM debate, no roles | No formal convergence, no refusal |
| **LLM Ensembles** | Majority vote of N independent LLMs | No structured argumentation |
| **MCTS + LLM (MCTSr)** | Tree search with LLM value function | No adversarial worlds |
| **Graph of Thoughts (GoT)** | Graph-structured LLM reasoning | No multi-world evaluation |


## 6.3 Quantitative Metrics


| Metric | Formula | What It Measures |
|--------|---------|------------------|
| **Refusal Accuracy** | correct_refuses / total_refuses | System refuses correctly on undecidable queries |
| **False Robustness Rate** | incorrect_ROBUST / total_ROBUST | How often system falsely claims something is safe |
| **False Fragility Rate** | incorrect_FRAGILE / total_FRAGILE | How often system falsely rejects a good option |
| **Adversarial Resilience** | 1 - (tricked / total_adversarial) | Resistance to trick queries |
| **Convergence Speed** | avg rounds to SPRT threshold | Efficiency of debate termination |
| **Solution Quality** (Mode B) | human-rated 1-5 scale | Quality of recommended solutions |
| **Cost Efficiency** | quality / total_tokens | Quality per unit of compute |
| **Pareto Distance** (Mode B) | geometric distance to true Pareto front | How close to theoretically optimal |


## 6.4 Ablation Studies (10 Experiments)


| ID | Ablation | Hypothesis |
|----|----------|-----------|
| A1 | Number of Worlds (1 vs 3 vs 5 vs 7) | 5 is the sweet spot for diversity/cost |
| A2 | Agents per World (1 vs 2 vs 3) | More agents = better convergence |
| A3 | With/Without QBAF (QBAF vs simple voting) | QBAF provides stronger formal guarantees |
| A4 | With/Without SPRT (SPRT vs fixed rounds) | SPRT converges faster |
| A5 | Minimax vs Average utility | U_min more robust than U_avg |
| A6 | With/Without Tools (ReAct vs pure reasoning) | Tools improve argument quality |
| A7 | Same LLM vs Mixed LLMs | Heterogeneous improves diversity |
| A8 | Evaluate vs Explore mode | Which finds better solutions? |
| A9 | Hierarchical vs Flat agents | Multi-layer improves quality |
| A10 | SPRT threshold sensitivity (α=β ∈ {0.001, 0.01, 0.05, 0.1}) | Strictness trade-off |


Each ablation: 50 queries, all 7 metrics, statistical significance via Mann-Whitney U test.


---


# 7. SCALING ANALYSIS


## 7.1 Complexity Formulas


```
Per-round cost:
  Intra-world QBAF: O(N × L²)   where L = arguments per world
  Inter-world QBAF: O(N²)
  LLM calls: N × M              (each sub-agent produces one argument)


Total LLM calls: O(N × M × R)   — dominant cost
Total QBAF compute: O(N × L² × R + N² × R)
Search overhead: O(b^d × R)     worst-case MCTS
```


## 7.2 Upper Bound on API Calls
```
Total calls = O(|W| × b^(d_m) × r)
  |W| = 5 worlds
  b = branching factor
  d_m = max depth
  r = debate rounds
```


**Mitigation**: Alpha-Beta pruning using intermediate QBAF scores at shallow depths → reduces effective branching factor to manageable constant → linear instead of exponential token scaling.


## 7.3 Practical Limits (Recommended)
```
N ≤ 5 worlds (more adds minimal diversity, heavy cost)
M ≤ 3 agents/world
R ≤ 15 rounds
K ≤ 10 candidate solutions in Explore mode
```

# 8. LOCAL MULTI-LLM BACKEND


## 8.1 LLM Options


| LLM | Size | Open Source | Local Run | Strength |
|-----|------|-------------|-----------|----------|
| **Llama 3.1** | 8B, 70B | Yes (Meta) | Ollama | Best all-rounder, factual |
| **Mistral** | 7B, 22B | Yes | Ollama | Strong reasoning, routing |
| **Gemma 2** | 9B, 27B | Yes (Google) | Ollama | Fast, efficient |
| **Qwen 2.5** | 7B, 72B | Yes (Alibaba) | Ollama | Strong math, multilingual |
| **Gemini** | — | No (API) | API only | Original model, baseline |


## 8.2 Multi-LLM Strategies


**Strategy A**: Same LLM for all agents → Tests architecture, not LLM diversity
**Strategy B**: Different LLM per world → Maximum diversity, tests LLM-agnosticism
**Strategy C**: Different LLM per role → Role-optimized selection


**X-MAS framework (2025)** validated: Heterogeneous LLM assignment yields **8-47% accuracy gains**.


## 8.3 Ollama Setup


```bash
# Install Ollama
curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh


# Pull models
ollama pull llama3.1:70b
ollama pull mistral:latest
ollama pull gemma2:27b
ollama pull qwen2.5:72b


# Structured JSON output (available since December 2024)
# Use Pydantic schemas for enforcement
# Set temperature=0 for deterministic output
# API endpoint: localhost:11434 (OpenAI-compatible)
```


## 8.4 Unified LLM Interface


```python
class LLMBackend:
    """Unified interface — swap LLMs without changing pipeline."""


    def __init__(self, provider, model_name):
        self.provider = provider  # "ollama", "gemini", "openai"
        self.model = model_name


    def generate(self, prompt, schema=None, temperature=0.7):
        if self.provider == "ollama":
            return self._call_ollama(prompt, schema)
        elif self.provider == "gemini":
            return self._call_gemini(prompt, schema)
        # Extensible for new providers


    def _call_ollama(self, prompt, schema):
        # POST localhost:11434/api/chat
        # With format=schema for structured output
        pass
```

# 9. KEY PAPERS — COMPLETE REFERENCE


| # | Paper | Year | Key Contribution | How PRDE Uses It |
|---|-------|------|-----------------|-----------------|
| 1 | Du et al. "Multiagent Debate" | 2023 | Multiple LLMs debate to improve factuality (+7.8 pts) and reasoning (+14.8 pts) | Baseline to beat. PRDE adds world isolation, QBAF, SPRT, refusal |
| 2 | Yao et al. "Tree of Thoughts" (NeurIPS) | 2023 | Multi-path exploration, BFS/DFS, backtracking. GPT-4: 4% → 74% on Game of 24 | Exploration agents use ToT for divergent search |
| 3 | Besta et al. "Graph of Thoughts" | 2023 | Arbitrary graph of thoughts, 62% quality↑, 31% cost↓ vs ToT | GoT aggregation: merge nodes when worlds converge |
| 4 | MCTSr "Monte Carlo Tree Self-Refine" | 2024 | MCTS + LLM, 5-stage loop, up to +16% accuracy on math | PRDE replaces LLM-as-judge with QBAF strength |
| 5 | Baroni et al. "Argumentation Semantics" | 2011 | Foundational QBAF theory, grounded/preferred semantics | Mathematical foundation for PRDE's core engine |
| 6 | CE-QArg "Counterfactual QBAF" | 2024 | Counterfactual explanations for argument strengths | Diagnostic tool: trace why solutions failed |
| 7 | Wald "Sequential Analysis" | 1945 | SPRT theory, optimal sample size, error bounds | Foundation for PRDE's debate termination |
| 8 | D3 "Debate, Deliberate, Decide" | 2024 | Role-specialized agents (advocates + judge + jury), budget-aware | Design pattern for PRDE's layered debate structure |
| 9 | HALO / AgentOrchestra | 2024/26 | Hierarchical multi-agent with MCTS for subtask planning | Inspiration for PRDE's 3-layer architecture |
| 10 | PAMA "Pareto Alignment" | 2024 | Multi-objective alignment via convex optimization, O(n) | Framework for Pareto front computation |
| 11 | Yao et al. "ReAct" | 2022 | Thought→Action→Observation loop for tool-using LLMs | Pattern for all PRDE tool-using sub-agents |
| 12 | MALBO "Multi-Agent Bayesian Opt" | 2024 | LLM-to-role assignment as multi-objective optimization, 65.8% cost reduction | Justifies heterogeneous LLM assignment |
| 13 | TalkHier "Structured Communication" | 2025 | Structured Layer 1→2 protocol (message + intermediate_output + background) | Communication protocol design for PRDE |


### Additional Papers to Cite
- Savage "Foundations of Statistics" (1951) — minimax regret theory
- Wald & Wolfowitz (1948) — SPRT optimality proof
- X-MAS "Heterogeneous LLM Agents" (2025) — 8-47% gains from diverse LLMs
- AgentLeak benchmark — 68.8% leakage rate in multi-agent messages
- scikit-fallback (2024) — selective prediction in ML
- Google ASPIRE — LLM selective prediction framework


---


# 10. RESEARCH CONTRIBUTIONS & GAP STATEMENT


## Gap Statement (QUOTE IN THESIS)


> "Existing multi-agent debate frameworks (Du et al., 2023) converge to consensus without formal convergence guarantees. Tree/Graph-of-Thoughts explore multiple paths but from a single model perspective without adversarial stress-testing (Yao et al., 2023; Besta et al., 2023). QBAF provides formal argumentation semantics but has not been applied to LLM-generated arguments or hierarchical multi-agent debates. Wald's SPRT provides optimal sequential testing but has not been used for debate termination in AI systems. **No existing system combines multi-world adversarial exploration, hierarchical multi-agent reasoning, formal argumentation graphs with proven convergence guarantees, statistical debate termination with bounded error rates, and first-class mathematical refusal.** PRDE v2.0 fills this gap."


## 5 Formal Contributions


| # | Contribution | Type | Novelty |
|---|-------------|------|---------|
| C1 | First system combining QBAF + SPRT + world isolation for multi-agent LLM decision evaluation | Architecture | HIGH |
| C2 | Mathematical definition of "epistemic refusal" with proven error bounds via SPRT | Theory | VERY HIGH |
| C3 | Explore-Debate-Converge (EDC) paradigm — multi-world solution exploration with Pareto selection | Paradigm | VERY HIGH |
| C4 | Hierarchical multi-agent debate with heterogeneous LLMs and formal convergence guarantees | System | HIGH |
| C5 | DecisionBench — first benchmark for evaluating AI refusal as correct outcome | Benchmark | HIGH |


## Connection to Selective Prediction (Bonus Positioning)
PRDE's "refusal" maps to the active ML research field of **Selective Prediction / Learning to Abstain**. PRDE's SPRT-based refusal is more principled than any existing threshold-based abstention method (scikit-fallback, ASPIRE, ASPEST).


---


# 11. OPEN PROBLEMS


| Problem | Description | Difficulty |
|---------|-------------|-----------|
| Dynamic Damping | Static d may over-attenuate deep arguments. Need localized, topology-aware d | Hard |
| d_intra vs d_inter | Optimal ratio between intra-world and inter-world damping — mathematically unresolved | Medium |
| Sub-Agent Conflict Resolution | How World Lead aggregates perfectly polarized sub-agent strengths without washing out minority risks | Medium |
| Semantic Sybil Attacks | Hundreds of near-identical support nodes to inflate scores. Need fast semantic deduplication | Hard |
| Continuous Action Spaces | Current QBAF optimized for discrete proposals. Adapting to continuous optimization (robotics, finance) | Hard |
| Non-i.i.d. SPRT | Debate rounds may be correlated. Extending SPRT guarantees to non-i.i.d. evidence | Hard |
| Adaptive World Selection | How many worlds is "enough"? When to add/remove worlds dynamically | Medium |
| Collusion Detection | Formally detecting subtle language-mediated collusion among agents | Hard |
| Human-in-the-Loop | How to integrate human feedback without breaking autonomy | Medium |
| Learning from History | Meta-learning from past queries to bias world generation or prune search | Medium |

# 16. CRITICAL FORMULAS — QUICK REFERENCE


### QBAF Strength Propagation
```
c(t+1) = τ + (1/d) × A × c(t)
c* = (I - (1/d)A)⁻¹ × τ
Convergence: ρ(A) < d
```


### SPRT Boundaries
```
A = log(β/(1-α)) ≈ -4.595   (accept H₀: ROBUST)
B = log((1-β)/α) ≈ +4.595   (accept H₁: FRAGILE)
LLR update: LLR += log[f_H1(u)/f_H0(u)]
```


### Beta Distribution PDFs
```
H₀: f(u; 5, 2) = u⁴(1-u) / B(5,2)    (robust, mean≈0.71)
H₁: f(u; 2, 5) = u(1-u)⁴ / B(2,5)    (fragile, mean≈0.29)
```


### Minimax Regret
```
R(s, w) = max_{s'} V(s', w) - V(s, w)
R_max(s) = max_w R(s, w)
s* = argmin_s R_max(s) = argmax_s min_w V(s, w)
```


### Pareto Dominance
```
s dominates s' iff:
  ∀w: strength(s, w) ≥ strength(s', w)
  ∃w: strength(s, w) > strength(s', w)
PF = {s | no s' dominates s}
```


### UCT (MCTS Selection)
```
UCT(vᵢ) = QBAF_Strength(vᵢ) + c × √(ln(N(parent)) / N(vᵢ))
```


### Refusal Trigger (Mode B)
```
If min_{s∈S} R_max(s) > ε_regret → REFUSE
```


### Convergence Speed
```
Total LLM calls: O(N × M × R)
QBAF per round: O(N × L²)
Search worst-case: O(b^d × R)
```
