# 17. META-ORCHESTRATOR STATE MACHINE


The Meta-Orchestrator is a **deterministic finite state machine**, NOT a generative LLM. Every transition is rule-based and auditable.


## 17.1 State Diagram


```
                    ┌──────────┐
                    │   INIT   │
                    └────┬─────┘
                         │ receive(user_query)
                         ▼
               ┌─────────────────────┐
               │ QUERY_CLASSIFICATION │
               │ • detect domain      │
               │ • estimate complexity │
               └─────────┬───────────┘
                         │ domain, complexity → config
                         ▼
               ┌─────────────────────┐
               │  WORLD_GENERATION   │
               │ • select N worlds    │
               │ • verify axiom       │
               │   incompatibility    │
               └─────────┬───────────┘
                         │ worlds[] validated
                         ▼
               ┌─────────────────────┐
               │ AGENT_INITIALIZATION│
               │ • spawn M agents/   │
               │   world              │
               │ • assign LLMs + tools│
               │ • allocate token     │
               │   budgets            │
               └─────────┬───────────┘
                         │ agents ready
                         ▼
               ┌─────────────────────┐
          ┌───→│    DEBATE_ROUND     │◄──────────────┐
          │    │ • sub-agents reason  │               │
          │    │ • world leads debate  │              │
          │    │ • collect arguments  │               │
          │    └─────────┬───────────┘               │
          │              │ arguments submitted        │
          │              ▼                            │
          │    ┌─────────────────────┐               │
          │    │ ARGUMENT_NORMALIZE  │               │
          │    │ • claim normalization│               │
          │    │ • duplicate detect   │               │
          │    │ • similarity filter  │               │
          │    └─────────┬───────────┘               │
          │              │ clean arguments            │
          │              ▼                            │
          │    ┌─────────────────────┐               │
          │    │    QBAF_UPDATE      │               │
          │    │ • update adjacency   │               │
          │    │ • check spectral ρ   │               │
          │    │ • iterate to fixed pt│               │
          │    └─────────┬───────────┘               │
          │              │ strengths converged         │
          │              ▼                            │
          │    ┌─────────────────────┐               │
          │    │    SPRT_CHECK       │               │
          │    │ • compute U_min      │               │
          │    │ • update LLR         │               │
          │    │ • check thresholds   │               │
          │    └─────────┬───────────┘               │
          │              │                            │
          │       ┌──────┼───────┐                   │
          │       ▼      ▼       ▼                   │
          │    ROBUST  FRAGILE  CONTINUE              │
          │       │      │       │                    │
          │       ▼      ▼       ▼                   │
          │    ┌─────┐ ┌─────┐ ┌─────────────┐      │
          │    │DONE │ │DONE │ │BUDGET_CHECK  │      │
          │    └─────┘ └─────┘ │ • round < R? │      │
          │                    │ • tokens < T? │      │
          │                    └──────┬────────┘      │
          │                      YES  │  NO           │
          │                    ┌──────┴──────┐        │
          │                    ▼             ▼        │
          │              next round     ┌────────┐    │
          └──────────────────┘          │ REFUSE │    │
                                        └────────┘
```


## 17.2 State Transition Table


| Current State | Event | Next State | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| INIT | receive(query) | QUERY_CLASSIFICATION | Parse input |
| QUERY_CLASSIFICATION | classified | WORLD_GENERATION | Set N, M, R, α, β |
| WORLD_GENERATION | worlds_valid | AGENT_INITIALIZATION | Generate world axioms |
| WORLD_GENERATION | axiom_conflict_fail | WORLD_GENERATION | Re-generate with constraints |
| AGENT_INITIALIZATION | agents_ready | DEBATE_ROUND | Assign LLMs, tools, budgets |
| DEBATE_ROUND | arguments_collected | ARGUMENT_NORMALIZE | Run normalization pipeline |
| ARGUMENT_NORMALIZE | clean_args | QBAF_UPDATE | Feed to QBAF engine |
| QBAF_UPDATE | strengths_converged | SPRT_CHECK | Compute U_min, update LLR |
| QBAF_UPDATE | spectral_radius_alert | QBAF_UPDATE | Increase d or prune cycles |
| SPRT_CHECK | LLR ≤ -4.595 | DONE(ROBUST) | Return verdict + explanation |
| SPRT_CHECK | LLR ≥ +4.595 | DONE(FRAGILE) | Return verdict + explanation |
| SPRT_CHECK | -4.595 < LLR < +4.595 | BUDGET_CHECK | Check remaining resources |
| BUDGET_CHECK | round < R AND tokens < T | DEBATE_ROUND | Continue debating |
| BUDGET_CHECK | round ≥ R OR tokens ≥ T | DONE(REFUSE) | Return REFUSE + LLR history |


## 17.3 Mode B State Extensions


```
After SPRT_CHECK in explore mode, add:


SPRT_CHECK → SOLUTION_MUTATION  (if current solutions all trending fragile)
SOLUTION_MUTATION → DEBATE_ROUND  (mutated solutions re-enter debate)


SPRT_CHECK → PARETO_FILTER  (if multiple solutions pass ROBUST threshold)
PARETO_FILTER → MINIMAX_SELECT  (pick from Pareto front)
MINIMAX_SELECT → DONE(OPTIMAL)  (return best solution)
```


## 17.4 Implementation Reference


```python
class OrchestratorState(Enum):
    INIT = "init"
    QUERY_CLASSIFICATION = "query_classification"
    WORLD_GENERATION = "world_generation"
    AGENT_INITIALIZATION = "agent_initialization"
    DEBATE_ROUND = "debate_round"
    ARGUMENT_NORMALIZE = "argument_normalize"
    QBAF_UPDATE = "qbaf_update"
    SPRT_CHECK = "sprt_check"
    BUDGET_CHECK = "budget_check"
    SOLUTION_MUTATION = "solution_mutation"  # Mode B only
    PARETO_FILTER = "pareto_filter"          # Mode B only
    MINIMAX_SELECT = "minimax_select"        # Mode B only
    DONE = "done"


class MetaOrchestrator:
    def __init__(self):
        self.state = OrchestratorState.INIT
        self.round = 0
        self.tokens_used = 0
        self.llr = 0.0
        self.config = None  # N, M, R, α, β


    def transition(self, event, data):
        """Deterministic state transition — NO LLM calls here."""
        old_state = self.state
        # ... transition logic per table above ...
        self.log_transition(old_state, self.state, event)
```

# 18. TOKEN BUDGET CONTROLLER


Without cost control, the system will explode. Every LLM call is metered.


## 18.1 Hard Budget Limits


```python
class TokenBudget:
    # === HARD LIMITS (never exceeded) ===
    MAX_AGENTS = 15               # N × M across all worlds
    MAX_ROUNDS = 15               # absolute debate round cap
    MAX_TOKENS_PER_AGENT = 500    # per agent per round (output)
    MAX_TOKENS_PER_PROMPT = 2000  # input context per agent
    MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS = 100_000    # entire pipeline budget


    # === SOFT LIMITS (trigger warnings) ===
    WARN_AT_PERCENT = 0.75        # warn at 75% budget consumed
    FORCE_DECISION_AT = 0.95      # force SPRT decision at 95%


    # === PER-COMPONENT ALLOCATION ===
    WORLD_GENERATION_BUDGET = 5_000   # tokens for world creation
    AGENT_INIT_BUDGET = 3_000         # tokens for agent setup
    DEBATE_BUDGET_PER_ROUND = 6_000   # N × M × 500 tokens/round
    SEARCH_BUDGET = 15_000            # MCTS/ToT exploration
```


## 18.2 Budget Enforcement Rules


```
Rule 1: Pre-flight check
  BEFORE spawning agents, calculate:
    estimated_cost = N × M × R × MAX_TOKENS_PER_AGENT
    if estimated_cost > MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS:
      reduce R, then M, then N until within budget


Rule 2: Per-round metering
  AFTER each debate round:
    tokens_used += sum(agent_tokens_this_round)
    if tokens_used > WARN_AT_PERCENT × MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS:
      log_warning("Budget 75% consumed, round {round}")
    if tokens_used > FORCE_DECISION_AT × MAX_TOTAL_TOKENS:
      force_sprt_decision()  # use current LLR to decide


Rule 3: Force decision protocol
  When budget exhausted:
    if LLR < -2.0 → lean ROBUST (with low-confidence flag)
    if LLR > +2.0 → lean FRAGILE (with low-confidence flag)
    else → REFUSE (budget exhausted, insufficient evidence)


Rule 4: Per-agent enforcement
  if agent.output_tokens > MAX_TOKENS_PER_AGENT:
    truncate at token limit
    log_warning(f"Agent {agent.id} truncated at {MAX_TOKENS_PER_AGENT}")
```


## 18.3 Cost Tracking Schema


```json
{
  "run_id": "run-2026-0308-001",
  "budget": {
    "total_allocated": 100000,
    "total_consumed": 67234,
    "remaining": 32766,
    "percent_used": 67.2
  },
  "per_round": [
    {"round": 1, "tokens": 5200, "agents_active": 15},
    {"round": 2, "tokens": 4800, "agents_active": 15},
    {"round": 3, "tokens": 4100, "agents_active": 12}
  ],
  "per_world": {
    "optimist": 14200,
    "pragmatist": 13800,
    "analyst": 12900,
    "strategist": 13100,
    "skeptic": 13234
  }
}
```

# 19. ARGUMENT NORMALIZATION PIPELINE


LLMs produce inconsistent, noisy arguments. Before QBAF ingestion, ALL arguments pass through a 4-stage normalization pipeline.


## 19.1 Pipeline Stages


```
Raw LLM Argument
      │
      ▼
┌──────────────────┐
│ Stage 1: SCHEMA  │  Validate JSON structure, required fields
│ VALIDATION       │  Reject malformed arguments
└────────┬─────────┘
         │
         ▼
┌──────────────────┐
│ Stage 2: CLAIM   │  Normalize claim text:
│ NORMALIZATION    │  • lowercase, strip whitespace
│                  │  • resolve coreferences ("it" → entity)
│                  │  • extract core proposition
└────────┬─────────┘
         │
         ▼
┌──────────────────┐
│ Stage 3: WEIGHT  │  Calibrate self-reported weights:
│ CALIBRATION      │  • clamp to [0.1, 0.95]
│                  │  • apply role-based scaling
│                  │  • penalize evidence-free claims
└────────┬─────────┘
         │
         ▼
┌──────────────────┐
│ Stage 4: DEDUP   │  → See Section 20 (Similarity Filter)
│ & SIMILARITY     │
└────────┬─────────┘
         │
         ▼
  Clean argument → QBAF Engine
```


## 19.2 Schema Validation Rules


```python
REQUIRED_FIELDS = ["claim", "evidence", "weight"]
OPTIONAL_FIELDS = ["attack_ids", "support_ids", "tool_results"]


def validate_argument(arg: dict) -> bool:
    # 1. Required fields present
    for field in REQUIRED_FIELDS:
        if field not in arg:
            return False


    # 2. Weight in valid range
    if not (0.0 <= arg["weight"] <= 1.0):
        return False


    # 3. Claim is non-empty string
    if not isinstance(arg["claim"], str) or len(arg["claim"]) < 10:
        return False


    # 4. Evidence is non-empty
    if not isinstance(arg["evidence"], str) or len(arg["evidence"]) < 20:
        return False


    return True
```


## 19.3 Weight Calibration Formula


```
calibrated_weight = clamp(raw_weight × role_factor × evidence_factor, 0.1, 0.95)


role_factor:
  Researcher     = 0.9   (exploratory, less certain)
  Data Analyst   = 1.0   (quantitative, calibrated)
  Risk Analyst   = 1.1   (critical analysis, slightly boosted)
  Simulator      = 0.95  (model-dependent)


evidence_factor:
  has_tool_results = 1.2  (grounded in external data)
  has_citations    = 1.1  (referenced sources)
  claim_only       = 0.7  (no backing evidence → penalized)
```


---


# 20. ARGUMENT SIMILARITY FILTER (Anti-Sybil)


Prevents semantic flooding where many near-identical arguments artificially inflate scores.


## 20.1 The Sybil Attack Problem


```
Without filter:
  Agent generates:
    arg1: "GDP will grow 6% due to tech boom"
    arg2: "Technology sector drives GDP growth to 6%"
    arg3: "Expected GDP growth: 6%, fueled by technology"
    arg4: "Tech-driven GDP expansion of approximately 6%"


  QBAF sees 4 separate support nodes → inflated strength
  This is a semantic Sybil attack.
```


## 20.2 Cosine Similarity Filter


```python
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer


SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD = 0.92  # merge if cosine > 0.92


class ArgumentSimilarityFilter:
    def __init__(self):
        self.encoder = SentenceTransformer('all-MiniLM-L6-v2')
        self.existing_embeddings = []  # (embedding, arg_id) pairs
        self.existing_claims = []


    def filter(self, new_argument: dict) -> dict:
        """Returns argument with merged flag, or None if duplicate."""
        new_emb = self.encoder.encode(new_argument["claim"])


        for i, (existing_emb, arg_id) in enumerate(self.existing_embeddings):
            similarity = cosine_similarity(new_emb, existing_emb)


            if similarity > SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD:
                # MERGE: keep existing, boost weight slightly
                merged_weight = min(
                    self.existing_claims[i]["weight"] + 0.05,
                    0.95
                )
                self.existing_claims[i]["weight"] = merged_weight
                self.existing_claims[i]["merge_count"] += 1
                return None  # reject duplicate


        # New unique argument — accept
        new_argument["merge_count"] = 1
        self.existing_embeddings.append((new_emb, new_argument["id"]))
        self.existing_claims.append(new_argument)
        return new_argument
```


## 20.3 Tiered Thresholds


| Similarity Range | Action | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| > 0.98 | **Reject** (exact duplicate) | Literal copy, no value |
| 0.92 – 0.98 | **Merge** (increment weight by +0.05) | Semantically identical, boosts slightly |
| 0.80 – 0.92 | **Flag** (log warning, allow) | Similar but potentially different nuance |
| < 0.80 | **Accept** (fully unique) | Distinct argument, full value |


## 20.4 Cross-World Similarity Check


Similarity filtering happens WITHIN each world. Cross-world similarity is EXPECTED (worlds may reach similar sub-conclusions). Only flag cross-world similarity if > 0.95 AND from different LLMs — this may indicate world leakage.


---


# 21. WORLD CONSISTENCY VALIDATOR


Worlds MUST be incompatible. This section formalizes the verification.


## 21.1 Axiom Schema


Each world declares a set of typed axioms:


```json
{
  "world_id": "optimist",
  "axioms": [
    {
      "dimension": "growth_rate",
      "operator": ">",
      "value": 5.0,
      "unit": "percent"
    },
    {
      "dimension": "risk_tolerance",
      "operator": ">",
      "value": 0.7,
      "unit": "scale_0_1"
    },
    {
      "dimension": "time_horizon",
      "operator": ">",
      "value": 5,
      "unit": "years"
    }
  ]
}
```


```json
{
  "world_id": "skeptic",
  "axioms": [
    {
      "dimension": "growth_rate",
      "operator": "<",
      "value": 2.0,
      "unit": "percent"
    },
    {
      "dimension": "risk_tolerance",
      "operator": "<",
      "value": 0.3,
      "unit": "scale_0_1"
    },
    {
      "dimension": "time_horizon",
      "operator": "<",
      "value": 2,
      "unit": "years"
    }
  ]
}
```


## 21.2 Pairwise Conflict Detection Algorithm


```python
def are_worlds_incompatible(world_a: dict, world_b: dict) -> bool:
    """Two worlds are incompatible if they conflict on ≥1 dimension."""
    conflicts = 0
    shared_dimensions = 0


    for axiom_a in world_a["axioms"]:
        for axiom_b in world_b["axioms"]:
            if axiom_a["dimension"] == axiom_b["dimension"]:
                shared_dimensions += 1
                if is_contradictory(axiom_a, axiom_b):
                    conflicts += 1


    # Must conflict on at least 1 shared dimension
    return conflicts >= 1


def is_contradictory(a: dict, b: dict) -> bool:
    """Check if two axioms on the same dimension are contradictory."""
    # "growth > 5%" contradicts "growth < 2%"
    if a["operator"] == ">" and b["operator"] == "<":
        return a["value"] > b["value"]  # e.g. 5 > 2 → conflict
    if a["operator"] == "<" and b["operator"] == ">":
        return b["value"] > a["value"]
    if a["operator"] == ">" and b["operator"] == ">":
        return False  # both optimistic, no conflict
    if a["operator"] == "==" and b["operator"] == "==":
        return a["value"] != b["value"]
    return False


def validate_all_worlds(worlds: list) -> bool:
    """Every pair of worlds must be incompatible."""
    for i in range(len(worlds)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(worlds)):
            if not are_worlds_incompatible(worlds[i], worlds[j]):
                raise WorldCompatibilityError(
                    f"Worlds {worlds[i]['world_id']} and "
                    f"{worlds[j]['world_id']} are NOT incompatible! "
                    f"Re-generate with stronger constraints."
                )
    return True
```


## 21.3 Maximum Shared Axiom Rule


```
No two worlds may share > 30% of their axiom values.
If worlds overlap by > 30%, they cannot provide genuine adversarial stress-testing.


overlap_ratio = shared_non_conflicting / total_axioms
if overlap_ratio > 0.30:
    reject_and_regenerate()
```

# 22. WORLD UTILITY FUNCTIONS


Each world has a DISTINCT utility function. Without these, worlds behave identically despite different axioms.


## 22.1 Utility Function Definitions


```python
class WorldUtility:
    """Each world evaluates solutions differently."""


    @staticmethod
    def optimist(solution: dict) -> float:
        """Maximize upside potential."""
        return (
            0.4 * solution["best_case_outcome"] +
            0.3 * solution["growth_potential"] +
            0.2 * solution["innovation_score"] +
            0.1 * solution["feasibility"]
        )


    @staticmethod
    def pragmatist(solution: dict) -> float:
        """Maximize feasibility and efficiency."""
        return (
            0.4 * solution["feasibility"] +
            0.3 * solution["cost_efficiency"] +
            0.2 * solution["implementation_speed"] +
            0.1 * solution["proven_track_record"]
        )


    @staticmethod
    def analyst(solution: dict) -> float:
        """Maximize data-driven support."""
        return (
            0.4 * solution["empirical_evidence_strength"] +
            0.3 * solution["quantitative_backing"] +
            0.2 * solution["statistical_significance"] +
            0.1 * solution["reproducibility"]
        )


    @staticmethod
    def strategist(solution: dict) -> float:
        """Maximize long-term strategic value."""
        return (
            0.4 * solution["long_term_optionality"] +
            0.3 * solution["systemic_impact"] +
            0.2 * solution["competitive_advantage"] +
            0.1 * solution["adaptability"]
        )


    @staticmethod
    def skeptic(solution: dict) -> float:
        """Minimize downside risk."""
        return (
            0.4 * (1.0 - solution["worst_case_severity"]) +
            0.3 * (1.0 - solution["failure_probability"]) +
            0.2 * solution["reversibility"] +
            0.1 * solution["tail_risk_coverage"]
        )
```


## 22.2 Utility Dimensions Required from Solutions


Every solution proposal must provide these evaluation dimensions:


```json
{
  "solution_id": "SOL-001",
  "evaluation_dimensions": {
    "best_case_outcome": 0.85,
    "growth_potential": 0.72,
    "innovation_score": 0.60,
    "feasibility": 0.90,
    "cost_efficiency": 0.78,
    "implementation_speed": 0.65,
    "proven_track_record": 0.55,
    "empirical_evidence_strength": 0.70,
    "quantitative_backing": 0.82,
    "statistical_significance": 0.68,
    "reproducibility": 0.75,
    "long_term_optionality": 0.60,
    "systemic_impact": 0.45,
    "competitive_advantage": 0.70,
    "adaptability": 0.80,
    "worst_case_severity": 0.30,
    "failure_probability": 0.25,
    "reversibility": 0.85,
    "tail_risk_coverage": 0.60
  }
}
```


## 22.3 Why This Matters


Without distinct utility functions:
```
Optimist evaluates: "Is solution good?" → 0.8
Skeptic evaluates:  "Is solution good?" → 0.4


Both just ask "is it good" with different personality → WEAK
```


With distinct utility functions:
```
Optimist evaluates: weighted_sum(best_case, growth, innovation) → 0.78
Skeptic evaluates:  weighted_sum(1-worst_case, 1-fail_prob, reversibility) → 0.52


They measure COMPLETELY DIFFERENT THINGS → STRONG adversarial tension
```

# 23. SOLUTION MUTATION ENGINE


Explore mode requires generating VARIED solutions. The mutation engine provides 6 distinct operators.


## 23.1 Mutation Operators


| Operator | What It Does | When Used |
|---|---|---|
| **MODIFY_ASSUMPTION** | Change one assumption in the reasoning path | When a specific assumption was attacked |
| **COMBINE_SOLUTIONS** | Cross two solutions, take best parts of each | When multiple partial solutions exist |
| **INVERT_STRATEGY** | Flip the core strategy (e.g., centralized → distributed) | When current direction is trending FRAGILE |
| **RELAX_CONSTRAINT** | Loosen a constraint that's blocking feasibility | When Pragmatist world rejects due to infeasibility |
| **ADD_SAFEGUARD** | Add risk mitigation without changing core strategy | When Skeptic world attacks on risk |
| **SUBSTITUTE_COMPONENT** | Replace one sub-component with an alternative | When a specific component is the weakness |


## 23.2 Mutation Engine Implementation


```python
class SolutionMutator:
    def __init__(self, llm_backend):
        self.llm = llm_backend
        self.mutation_history = []  # track what was tried


    def mutate(self, solution: dict, attack: dict,
               mutation_type: str) -> dict:
        """Generate a mutated solution based on a specific attack."""


        prompts = {
            "MODIFY_ASSUMPTION": (
                f"The following solution was attacked on assumption "
                f"'{attack['target_node']}':\n{solution}\n"
                f"Attack: {attack['argument']}\n"
                f"Generate a modified solution that changes ONLY "
                f"this assumption to address the attack."
            ),
            "COMBINE_SOLUTIONS": (
                f"Combine the strongest parts of these two solutions:\n"
                f"Solution A: {solution}\n"
                f"Solution B: {attack.get('alternative')}\n"
                f"Create a hybrid that retains the best of both."
            ),
            "INVERT_STRATEGY": (
                f"The following solution is trending FRAGILE:\n"
                f"{solution}\n"
                f"Generate an alternative that takes the OPPOSITE "
                f"strategic approach while addressing the same problem."
            ),
            "RELAX_CONSTRAINT": (
                f"This solution was rejected for infeasibility:\n"
                f"{solution}\n"
                f"Identify the tightest constraint and relax it. "
                f"Explain the trade-off of relaxation."
            ),
            "ADD_SAFEGUARD": (
                f"This solution has risk vulnerability:\n"
                f"{solution}\n"
                f"Risk: {attack['argument']}\n"
                f"Add a specific safeguard WITHOUT changing core strategy."
            ),
            "SUBSTITUTE_COMPONENT": (
                f"This solution's component '{attack['target_node']}' "
                f"is weak:\n{solution}\n"
                f"Replace it with an alternative component."
            ),
        }


        mutated = self.llm.generate(
            prompts[mutation_type],
            schema=SolutionSchema  # enforce structured output
        )
        mutated["mutation_from"] = solution["solution_id"]
        mutated["mutation_type"] = mutation_type
        self.mutation_history.append(mutated)
        return mutated
```


## 23.3 Mutation Selection Strategy


```
After each debate round in Explore mode:


1. For each solution trending FRAGILE (LLR > +2.0):
   a. Identify the STRONGEST attack against it
   b. Select mutation operator based on attack type:
      - attack_type "empirical_refutation"  → MODIFY_ASSUMPTION
      - attack_type "feasibility_challenge" → RELAX_CONSTRAINT
      - attack_type "risk_exposure"         → ADD_SAFEGUARD
      - attack_type "strategic_weakness"    → INVERT_STRATEGY
      - attack_type "component_failure"     → SUBSTITUTE_COMPONENT
   c. Generate mutated solution
   d. Insert into next debate round


2. If ALL solutions trending fragile:
   → COMBINE top 2 solutions (crossover)
   → Generate 1 INVERTED solution (diversity injection)
```

# 24. EXPERIMENTAL LOGGING SYSTEM


Without structured logs, you CANNOT publish a paper. Every experiment must produce reproducible, analyzable data.


## 24.1 Log Structure


```
experiment_logs/
├── run_{timestamp}/

│   │   ├── round_001.json       # all arguments, attacks, supports
│   │   ├── round_002.json
│   │   └── ...
│   ├── qbaf_snapshots/
│   │   ├── qbaf_round_001.json  # adjacency matrix + strengths
│   │   ├── qbaf_round_002.json
│   │   └── ...
│   ├── sprt_history.json        # LLR trajectory per round
│   ├── search_tree.json         # MCTS/ToT tree (Mode B)
│   ├── mutations.json           # solution mutations (Mode B)
│   ├── token_usage.json         # per-agent, per-round token counts
│   ├── verdict.json             # final decision + explanation
│   └── audit_hash.json          # SHA-256 chain for verification
```

| World axioms | Verify incompatibility | Axiom sets per world |
| Search tree (Mode B) | Visualize exploration | Node-edge structure with UCT values |
| Mutation history (Mode B) | Trace solution evolution | Mutation type + source + result |
| Final verdict + reasoning | Thesis results section | Structured verdict JSON |

# 27. STATISTICAL EVALUATION (TO BE FILLED)


> ⚠️ **STATUS**: Requires experimental results first.


## 27.1 Required Statistical Tests


| Test | Purpose | When to Use |
|------|---------|-------------|
| **Mann-Whitney U** | Compare PRDE vs each baseline | Non-parametric, doesn't assume normality |
| **Wilcoxon Signed-Rank** | Paired comparison on same queries | When same queries run on all systems |
| **Bootstrap CI** | Confidence intervals for metrics | 95% CI on all reported metrics |
| **McNemar's Test** | Compare binary outcomes (correct/incorrect) | Per-query accuracy comparison |
| **Bonferroni Correction** | Multiple comparisons correction | When testing 6+ baselines simultaneously |


## 27.2 Results Template


```
PRDE vs CoT:
  Mann-Whitney U = —, p = —
  Effect size (Cohen's d) = —
  95% CI for Refusal Accuracy difference: [—, —]


PRDE vs MAD (Du et al.):
  Mann-Whitney U = —, p = —
  Effect size (Cohen's d) = —
  95% CI for Refusal Accuracy difference: [—, —]


[... repeat for all baselines ...]
```


## 27.3 Significance Threshold


- **p < 0.05** for all primary claims
- **p < 0.01** for headline claims (refusal accuracy, adversarial resilience)
- Report **effect sizes**, not just p-values (reviewers demand this)

# 28. FAILURE ANALYSIS (TO BE FILLED)


> ⚠️ **STATUS**: Requires experimental results. Every research paper MUST show where the system fails.


## 28.1 Expected Failure Categories


| Category | Description | Why It Could Happen |
|----------|-------------|---------------------|
| **False ROBUST** | System says ROBUST when ground truth is FRAGILE | All worlds miss a hidden tail risk |
| **False FRAGILE** | System rejects a genuinely good solution | Skeptic world too aggressive, dominates debate |
| **False REFUSE** | System refuses when a clear answer exists | SPRT oscillates, never crosses threshold |
| **World Collapse** | All worlds converge to same conclusion | Axiom generation produced near-identical worlds |
| **Sybil Bypass** | Semantic flooding inflates scores despite filter | Threshold too lenient, or adversarial paraphrasing |
| **Budget Exhaustion** | Forced decision is wrong | Complex query needs more rounds than budget allows |
| **Tool Hallucination** | Tool returns garbage, agent trusts it | No validation on tool outputs |


## 28.2 Failure Case Template


```
FAILURE CASE #X: [Title]
  Query: "..."
  Ground Truth: ROBUST / FRAGILE / UNDECIDABLE
  PRDE Output: [wrong answer]
  Root Cause: [what went wrong]
  SPRT Trajectory: [...]
  Which world failed: [...]
  Proposed Fix: [...]
```


## 28.3 Failure Rate Targets


| Category | Acceptable Rate | Action if Exceeded |
|----------|----------------|-------------------|
| False ROBUST | < 2% | Increase skeptic world weight |
| False FRAGILE | < 5% | Reduce damping factor |
| False REFUSE | < 10% | Increase max rounds |
| Total error | < 5% | System-wide investigation |

## 1 — Original Vision


### The Core Problem


> "I want to do everything but sometimes motivation or laziness stops me. First I thought to make this for myself but every student and every hard working person needs this."


### What I Want to Build


An **AI-powered planner** that doesn't just schedule — it **observes, adapts, motivates, and holds accountable**. It should feel like a strict but caring personal coach who knows you deeply.


### Core Ideas (As Originally Proposed)


#### Smart Scheduling


- App automatically **plans full day schedule first**, then user can make adjustments
- Before planning, ask:
  - What do you want to do?
  - How many hours can you give for each task in a day?
  - Is there anything you usually do in a day? (breakfast, lunch, snacks, dinner, etc.)
- After subtracting routine time → fill the rest with tasks intelligently


#### Observation Week (First 7 Days)


- For the entire first week, the system **observes the user's real behavior**
- Every **30 minutes**: ask "What are you doing? Are you doing this task or something else?"
- Every **hour**: ask "Did you only do this task in this time period or also anything else?"
- The system **notes everything in memory**
- After the week → the planner adjusts itself based on real user patterns
- Every day after that, it keeps learning and adjusting


#### End-of-Day Review


- At end of every day, ask: "Did you only do what was planned, or also something else?"
- Use this data to continuously improve the planner


#### Cross-Device Monitoring (Mobile + PC)


- App is linked with mobile
- **Draws over other apps** (Android overlay)
- Monitors app activity — not detailed content, just:
  - Which app was opened
  - When it was opened
  - How long it was used
- At end of day, the system reviews all app usage and asks:
  - "Why did you open this app?"
  - Options: Productive (education/research/GK) | Personal reasons | Timepass
- Categorizes and calculates productive vs unproductive time


#### Smart Screen Time Alerts


- When user exceeds allocated time for social media/personal apps:
  - Pop-up appears on screen telling user to leave and do the scheduled task
  - Includes **motivation** + reminds what needs to be done
  - Shows: "If you skip → this happens. If you continue → this happens."
  - **Extra option**: "Need a little more time? How much? 10m / 15m / Custom"
- After extended time is over, planner **adjusts the entire schedule**
- Gives choice:
  - Deduct extra time **evenly from all remaining tasks**
  - Deduct from **one specific task** (user picks)


#### Task Setup — The Deep Questions


At the start, for every task, ask:


- **Why** do you want to do this?
- How **serious** are you about this? (1-10)
- How much **can** you do this? Rate 1-10
- **Priority** level
- If multiple tasks have same seriousness, extra time reduces from the **low priority** task


These "why" answers serve two purposes:


1. **Motivation fuel** — remind user of their own reasons when they want to quit
2. **Consequence reminders** — if you don't do this, here's what happens


#### The Basketball Example (Motivation Style)


> "A task: basketball, seriousness level 10, training time 2 hours. When it's time to motivate, the system reminds: 'If you don't train, after 10 days when the tournament comes, you'll get humiliated in front of people by your weak dribble. You'll lose all respect. Your crush is watching.' — That's why the system asks WHY you want to do each task."


#### Truthfulness System


- At beginning of each task: "Are you doing this task? Say truthfully. If you lie, you're not lying to me — you're lying to yourself, lying to your hopes."
- If user says No → ask the reason
- Reason validity depends on seriousness:
  - **Seriousness 10 task**, reason = "not in mood / want to play games / watch movie"  
    → **REJECTED** — these are excuses. Motivate hard.
  - Lower seriousness → more flexible with reasons


#### Smart Priority Decisions


- If user is studying (high priority) and a low priority task notification comes:
  - If user declines the low priority task to continue studying → **acceptable, praised**
  - If user wants extra time from low priority task for study → **granted, because study > low priority**
- This is why **LLM thinking is needed** — to make these context-aware, human-like decisions in real-time


#### LLM Integration


> "I want to add AI because it will be easy for the software to plan and give motivation and think from the user's perspective. We have to add an LLM."


The AI handles:


- Planning and schedule generation
- Motivation personalization
- Excuse analysis and reframing
- Real-time priority decisions
- Adaptive behavior understanding


---


## 2 — Expert Review & Refinements


### Three AI Layers (Not Just LLM)


The reviewer identified that the system needs **3 distinct AI layers**:


| Layer                          | Purpose                                                                                                     | Technology            |
| ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------- |
| **Behavioral Tracking Engine** | App usage, time tracking, screen overlay, productivity categorization                                       | System APIs, OS hooks |
| **Rule-Based Decision Engine** | If seriousness=10 AND reason="not in mood" → reject excuse → trigger motivation                             | Logical engine (code) |
| **LLM Layer**                  | Personalized motivation, excuse reframing, adaptive scheduling, psychological coaching, dynamic negotiation | OpenAI / Gemini API   |


> **LLM = Brain. Rule Engine = Backbone. Tracking System = Eyes.**


### Motivation Mode System (Refined)


Instead of pure fear-based motivation, refined into **3 modes × 4 intensities**:


#### Modes


1. **🌱 Growth Mode** (Default)  
   "You chose this because you want to become elite. Act like it."


2. **⚡ Emotional Mode** (High Intensity)  
   "You rated this a 10. That means this matters deeply. If you skip now, you are not breaking a task — you are breaking your own word."


3. **🧠 Logical Mode** (Pure Rational)  
   "Skipping this reduces weekly goal completion by 18%. That increases backlog by 2.3 hours."


#### Intensity Slider


`Soft → Balanced → Intense → Brutal`  
User chooses their preference. Can be different per task.


### Identity Mode


User selects who they want to become:


- 🎓 Student / AIR <100 Aspirant
- 💻 FAANG SDE
- 🏀 Athlete
- 🚀 Startup Founder
- 🧠 AI Researcher
- ✏️ Custom Identity


System adapts: motivation tone, task structure, time expectations, sleep discipline, distraction tolerance.


### Future Self Simulator


Every 30 days, AI generates:


- **Version A** → "If you stay disciplined, in 90 days you'll be..."
- **Version B** → "If you continue current inconsistency..."
- Visual projection of skill growth, discipline trend, burnout risk


### Discipline Score System


Formula:


- Planned vs Completed: 30%
- Distraction time ratio: 20%
- Excuse frequency: 15%
- Seriousness mismatch: 15%
- Consistency streak: 20%


Daily score: 0–100 | Weekly average | Monthly graph


### Weekly Brutal Review (Every Sunday)


System asks:


- Where did you lie to yourself?
- Which excuses repeated?
- Which seriousness-10 tasks were skipped?
- What patterns caused distraction?
- What is the one weakness this week?


Then provides: pattern summary, weakness report, improvement strategy.


### Psychological Design Decision


> Fear-based motivation works short-term but causes anxiety burnout.  
> Better approach: **Identity-based discipline + controlled emotional pressure.**  
> Instead of "You'll get humiliated" → "You chose this because you want to become someone unstoppable. Are you acting like that version right now?"


User's response: "Sometimes you need emotional type of motivation, so I want to do it my way too." → **Solution**: User controls intensity. Both approaches are available.

## 3 — Final Feature Set


### Core Features


- [x] Auto-schedule generation based on tasks + routines
- [x] Deep task setup (why, seriousness, priority, consequences, flexibility)
- [x] Observation Week (7-day behavioral learning)
- [x] 30-min check-ins + hourly summaries
- [x] End-of-day review
- [x] Truthfulness system with excuse analysis
- [x] 3 motivation modes × 4 intensities
- [x] Adaptive time negotiation (extend → choose where to cut)
- [x] Identity Mode (Student/Athlete/Founder/etc.)
- [x] Cross-device monitoring (eventually mobile + PC)
- [x] Smart screen time alerts with overlay popups
- [x] App usage categorization (productive/personal/timepass)
- [x] LLM integration for personalization
- [x] Smart priority decisions in real-time
- [x] Seriousness-based excuse rejection


### Analytics & Gamification


- [x] Discipline Score (daily/weekly/monthly)
- [x] Weekly Brutal Review
- [x] Future Self Simulator (90-day projections)
- [x] Productivity charts and trends


### Additional Features (From Review)


- [x] Energy Level Tracker — schedule hard tasks during peak energy
- [x] Habit Stacking — link new tasks to existing habits
- [x] Accountability Partner Mode — share scores with a friend
- [x] Emergency Reschedule — "my day went off-rails" one-tap
- [x] Focus Mode — timed deep work with distraction blocking
- [x] Sleep Quality Input — morning check adjusts day expectations
- [x] Micro-Wins — celebrate small completions with animations
- [x] Context Switching Penalty — warn when toggling tasks too much
- [x] Procrastination Detector — intervene when doing low-priority while high is pending
- [x] Voice Check-ins — optional voice responses (future)

## 4 — System Architecture


```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    USER DEVICES                      │
│                                                     │
│   ┌──────────────┐          ┌──────────────┐        │
│   │  Web App      │          │  Mobile App   │       │
│   │  (Vite+React) │  ◄────► │  (React Native)│      │
│   └──────┬───────┘          └──────┬───────┘        │
│          │                         │                 │
│   ┌──────┴───────┐          ┌──────┴───────┐        │
│   │ Desktop Track │          │ Mobile Track  │       │
│   │ (Electron)    │          │ (Android API) │       │
│   └──────┬───────┘          └──────┬───────┘        │
└──────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────────┘
           │                         │
           ▼                         ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   CORE ENGINE                        │
│                                                     │
│   ┌────────────┐  ┌────────────┐  ┌────────────┐   │
│   │ Scheduler   │  │ Rule Engine │  │ Behavior   │   │
│   │ Engine      │  │             │  │ Tracker    │   │
│   └─────┬──────┘  └─────┬──────┘  └─────┬──────┘   │
│         │               │               │           │
│         ▼               ▼               ▼           │
│   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
│   │         LLM Brain (Gemini/OpenAI)        │       │
│   │  • Motivation Generator                  │       │
│   │  • Excuse Analyzer                       │       │
│   │  • Schedule Optimizer                    │       │
│   │  • Future Self Simulator                 │       │
│   └─────────────────────────────────────────┘       │
│                                                     │
│   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐       │
│   │    Data Layer (IndexedDB / Cloud Sync)   │       │
│   └─────────────────────────────────────────┘       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```


### Tech Stack


| Component        | Technology                             |
| ---------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| Web Frontend     | Vite + React + React Router            |
| Styling          | Vanilla CSS (dark mode, glassmorphism) |
| Icons            | Lucide React                           |
| Animations       | Framer Motion                          |
| Date Handling    | date-fns                               |
| Local Storage    | IndexedDB (via idb library)            |
| LLM API          | OpenAI GPT / Google Gemini             |
| Desktop Tracking | Electron (future)                      |
| Mobile App       | React Native (future)                  |
| Cloud Backend    | Firebase / Supabase (future)           |

## 5 — Implementation Plan


### Phase 1 — Project Setup & Core UI Shell


- Initialize Vite + React project
- Design system (CSS variables, dark mode, glassmorphism, animations)
- App shell with sidebar navigation
- First-launch detection → onboarding redirect


### Phase 2 — Onboarding & Task Setup


- Multi-step onboarding wizard (identity, routine, first tasks, motivation style)
- Task creation with all deep questions
- IndexedDB persistence


### Phase 3 — Smart Scheduler Engine


- Auto-scheduling algorithm (priority × seriousness × deadline)
- Timeline view with drag-and-drop
- Time negotiation system (4 options for extending)


### Phase 4 — Active Monitoring & Check-ins


- 30-min check-in popups
- Hourly summaries
- End-of-day review
- Observation Week mode
- Truthfulness system


### Phase 5 — Motivation Engine


- 3 modes × 4 intensities
- Dynamic content from task "why" + identity + behavior patterns
- Excuse analyzer with seriousness-based rejection


### Phase 6 — LLM Integration


- API integration (user provides key)
- Personalized motivation generation
- Excuse analysis and reframing
- Schedule optimization suggestions
- Future Self narratives


### Phase 7 — Analytics & Gamification


- Discipline Score system
- Weekly Brutal Review
- Future Self Simulator
- Productivity charts


### Phase 8 — Desktop Activity Tracking (Future)


- Electron wrapper
- Window tracking + categorization
- Overlay notifications


### Phase 9 — Mobile & Cloud Sync (Future)


- React Native companion app
- Firebase/Supabase backend
- Android overlay service


---


## 6 — Loopholes & Solutions


| #   | Loophole                                   | Risk   | Solution                                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------ | ------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | User lies during check-ins                 | High   | Honesty score tracking; LLM detects inconsistencies; show impact of lying on projections |
| 2   | User ignores/dismisses popups              | High   | Escalation: gentle → firm → full-screen; track dismissal rate; adapt frequency           |
| 3   | User games seriousness levels              | Medium | AI cross-references behavior with stated seriousness; weekly review flags mismatches     |
| 4   | Schedule too rigid → burnout               | High   | Built-in flex time; energy-aware scheduling; fatigue pattern detection                   |
| 5   | Motivation fatigue (same messages)         | Medium | LLM varies messages; rotate modes; adapt intensity based on user response                |
| 6   | User sets unrealistic schedules            | Medium | System flags if >14 productive hours; suggests min breaks based on identity              |
| 7   | Extended time abuse (always extending)     | Medium | Limit to 3 extensions/day; track frequency; show cumulative impact                       |
| 8   | Observation week data is messy             | Low    | LLM normalizes data; asks clarifying questions post-week                                 |
| 9   | User uninstalls when frustrated            | High   | Gentle onboarding; adjustable intensity; "this is hard" acknowledgment mode              |
| 10  | Privacy concerns with tracking             | High   | All data local by default; explicit consent for cloud; clear data anytime                |
| 11  | Different weekday vs weekend patterns      | Medium | Separate weekday/weekend schedules; detect patterns per day-of-week                      |
| 12  | Unexpected events (sick days, emergencies) | Medium | Emergency reschedule button; "health day" mode that pauses scoring                       |
| 13  | Multiple users on same device              | Low    | Profile system with PIN/password                                                         |
| 14  | App usage tracking battery drain           | Medium | Optimize polling intervals; respect battery saver modes                                  |

## 8 — Conversation Archive


### Conversation 1 — Initial Vision (March 2, 2026)


**Context**: First conversation where the entire project was conceived. User described the complete vision for an AI-powered discipline system.


**Key Decisions Made**:


1. System will observe user for first 7 days before creating optimized schedule
2. Three AI layers: Tracking Engine, Rule Engine, LLM Brain
3. Motivation has 3 modes (Growth/Emotional/Logical) × 4 intensities
4. User controls motivation intensity — both identity-based and emotional approaches preserved
5. Identity Mode allows the system to adapt to user's goals (Student/Athlete/Founder/etc.)
6. Cross-device monitoring (mobile + PC) planned as future phase
7. LLM integration chosen for: motivation, excuse analysis, schedule optimization, future simulation
8. Tech stack: Vite + React (web), Electron (desktop), React Native (mobile)


**Conversation ID**: `7a91ac8d-d1e6-4ef6-9b63-a1d5c9b09cb6`
